This localizes the current failure to Telethon iterator initialization/first iteration, after successful connect and authorization, before message conversion.
- Root cause identified from production code + Telethon iterator semantics: `TelethonMtprotoTransport.fetch_history` forwards optional `min_message_id=None` / `max_message_id=None` directly as Telethon `min_id=None` / `max_id=None`. Telethon v1 message iteration uses numeric comparisons such as `max(offset_id, max_id)` (or `max(offset_id, min_id)` for reverse iteration), so explicit `None` can raise `TypeError` before the first history message is yielded. This exactly matches M4AO2 and explains why M4AH3 could pass when the optional bounds were omitted instead of explicitly passed as None. Required fix: normalize absent bounds to Telethon's numeric sentinel 0 at the transport boundary, with focused regressions for initial, incremental, and backfill calls.

- Telegram MTProto M4AY1 review of commit `443dc8c91bd7e66f5c24fdf8ab0f2bd270402673`: NOT YET ACCEPTED. The one-page/limit-20 scope control flow is correct, but fresh scope bootstrap still computes the legacy 14-day cutoff and passes it into `_normalize_entry`. Therefore a quiet chat whose latest 20 provider entries are older than 14 days can fetch exactly 20 entries yet materialize none of them, contradicting the selected count-based bootstrap product intent. The implementation also persists that synthetic 14-day cutoff on a fresh scope row even though scope bootstrap is intended to be count-bounded, not time-bounded. No deploy is authorized. Corrective M4AY1R must make scope-mode normalization independent of the legacy 14-day cutoff and leave/preserve `history_cutoff_at` rather than introducing a time window, while keeping manual `sync_group` behavior unchanged.
- Telegram MTProto M4AY1R scope count-bound correction: BUILT / LOCALLY REVIEWED ONLY. Scope-mode normalization now uses an aware `datetime.min` cutoff, so fresh and incremental scope entries are not rejected by the manual 14-day window; scope persistence preserves existing `history_cutoff_at`, backfill cursor, and completion state, and fresh scope does not create a synthetic cutoff. Manual `sync_group` remains unchanged. The DB-independent cutoff regression passes with `SKIP_DB_FIXTURES=1`; the DB-backed A3/A4.2/A4.4/Q1 matrix remains blocked at setup because local `db` host resolution is unavailable. No schema/Alembic/provider/AI/recurring behavior changes were made.

- Telegram MTProto M4AY1R review of corrective commit `e0f89a30bb204b904bb49e47974dc4dbba5bbb48`: NOT YET ACCEPTED. The normalization cutoff is correctly non-time-limiting in scope mode, but fresh scope persistence still executes `selection.history_cutoff_at = cutoff` because the condition only skips persistence when `scope_mode=true` AND a previous latest-message cursor already exists. For a fresh scope row, this writes aware `datetime.min` into `history_cutoff_at`, contradicting the required "fresh scope leaves cutoff unset/preserved" semantics. This is also a cross-mode hazard: if the same row is later manually selected, manual `sync_group` will treat persisted `datetime.min` as its existing cutoff and can backfill arbitrarily deep instead of using the intended 14-day manual window. No deploy is authorized.

Human-shell run on production runtime/ref `c69d2353c19c4958e1fb60aac69466fcf6ac1482`, Alembic `0046`, passed repo/ref/worktree/Compose/DB/API/worker/DB-health guards. - Retirement plan Stage A — code/runtime isolation: first release a guarded code change that stops accepting `/telegram/link` and Bot webhook ingress, disables Telegram entries in `ConnectionStatusService`/legacy account UI, and rejects Telegram Bot send/reply/action routing while leaving MTProto routes, recurring sync, history, visibility, and `TELEGRAM_MTPROTO_AI_ENABLED=false` unchanged. - Retirement plan Stage C — cleanup: after a retention decision and compatibility window, remove Bot-only routes, `TelegramHttpTransport`, webhook service, link/account stores, CLI webhook setup, legacy client connect/status models and UI, Bot-only tests/docs/config entries, and only then consider a separately authorized destructive schema migration for `telegram_accounts`/`telegram_link_states`. - Telegram Bot API M4BS1 first final Stage C live-verifier attempt: SAFE LOCAL BLOCK / NO PRODUCTION ACCESS. Wrapper stopped at `M4BR1_BLOCKED=local_production_ref` before target parsing/SSH, so production SSH=0, provider calls=0, DB/env writes=0, and service recreation=0. GitHub confirms canonical `production` still points to exact accepted runtime `bd1433921a056b8dc42bd23c6ecf0e4ce2bedf4b`. Root cause is verifier ref-validation logic: both local wrapper and remote helper use explicit `git fetch ... production` followed by `rev-parse origin/production`; an explicit fetch ref without a destination does not reliably refresh the remote-tracking ref, so a stale `origin/production` can false-fail. Corrective direction: use authoritative read-only `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/production` (and analogous main freshness where needed) rather than relying on local tracking-ref mutation. The authorized one-shot run is consumed; no retry is authorized until corrected code is reviewed and fresh human authorization is given.

Telegram `message_edited` and `message_deleted` transport-event notifications remain unchanged for now. No production change is yet authorized.

- Telegram MTProto post-transport closure audit: ordinary transport/communication capability is substantially complete (receive/sync, compose send, reply via message anchor, outbound edit, exact-message delete, mark-read; external writes remain approval/pending-action-plan gated). Two concrete implementation gaps were found: (1) current MTProto objects materialize metadata as `peer_id/peer_title/sender_peer_id/topic_id`, while the generic Telegram conversation projection expects legacy `chat_id/chat_display_name/from_user_id`, so MTProto messages may stay singleton and miss conversation-stack summarization; (2) `chat_message` is absent from correlation trigger kinds, so an MTProto message does not currently initiate automatic task/object correlation even when the Telegram AI gate is enabled. A local synthetic false->true integration proof is required before the Telegram topic is considered development-complete. No production AI enablement is authorized.

- Architect review of `10cb4a3179e2a4c1acdfe94c3814a8aaf9ec7694`: conversation projection, narrow MTProto correlation trigger, false->true catch-up, label path, assistant/context gate, stack summary and CRUD proof are accepted. One blocking semantic defect remains before final Telegram development closure: MTProto participation currently labels every inbound message from a non-self sender as `direct_recipient`, including group/supergroup peers. That overstates personal relevance and can incorrectly promote group temporal statements into the user's consolidated calendar. Required correction: only private inbound may infer direct-recipient from direction; group/supergroup must use non-direct group/channel semantics, and MTProto `sender_peer_id`/`peer_kind` must participate in temporal source signatures. No production change is authorized.

- Telegram MTProto temporal participation correction: BUILT / LOCALLY VERIFIED ONLY. Outbound MTProto from the connected user remains `sender`. Only inbound `peer_kind=private` from another sender is `direct_recipient`. Group and supergroup inbound are not personal addressing and use the existing non-direct channel policy, so a high-confidence temporal statement stays `possible` and is not promoted to `expected`. Missing or unknown peer kind does not infer personal addressing. MTProto temporal source signatures include `sender_peer_id`, `peer_kind`, and `direction`. Production `TELEGRAM_MTPROTO_AI_ENABLED` remains false. No production SSH, provider call, or production mutation.

- Telegram rehearsal attempt `tgprod0922a` exit-status capture: `REHEARSAL_WRAPPER_EXIT=1`; the user pressed an additional empty Enter before the capture, which does not normally execute a shell command and therefore does not invalidate the prior status. - Architect review of `7d571f410a31e4fc322f90ae0ac5e976b300b4fb`: PARTIAL ACCEPT / NOT LIVE-READY. Removing unsupported `compose run --no-build` is correct and `--pull never` prevents registry pulls, but it does not establish a hard no-build guarantee for a service defined with `build:`. Current Compose `run` internals retain a build-capable create path unless an internal no-build condition is set, and `run` exposes no public `--no-build` option. The running-container image-id check does not bind the subsequent `compose run api` to that exact image. The active corrective task therefore replaces one-shot container creation with stdin-fed `docker compose exec -T ... api python3 -B -` inside the already-running API container, which launches a separate process with exec-only environment overrides and requires no image create/build/pull path. (1) Inbox rapid deletion UX: mobile swipe-to-remove and desktop inline trash-after-Ask-Secretary/Open-in-Graph are already implemented/live, so treat them as regression verification and fix only any current mismatch. (3) Prompt-injection defense-in-depth: do not build a new filter/security subsystem. Interactive Assistant/finalization, proactive review, and temporal extractor/matcher already classify stored/external content as untrusted data/evidence and forbid following embedded instructions. Extend the same short invariant only to remaining background generative prompts that consume external/object text (notably resource/conversation summarization, correlation judge, and auto-label object payload); preserve all existing approval/tool authorization gates and do not strip source content. - Immediate post-rollout micro-fix authorized next: collapsed/expanded Inbox Conversation Stack cards must surface bookmark presence from the exact child messages represented by that rendered stack. This is a Flutter-only derived presentation signal: reuse already-loaded child bookmark state, do not create/persist a bookmark for the stack itself. One unique child bookmark color => one filled bookmark glyph; multiple distinct colors => compact unique-color glyph summary (bounded to avoid layout growth) plus overflow indication/semantics as needed. The aggregate is read-only and must update immediately when a child bookmark is added/changed/cleared.

- Future backlog, not part of the bookmark micro-fix: Conversation Stack label summary. Preferred first implementation is a deterministic deduplicated union of labels already assigned to the represented child messages, rendered through the existing `ObjectLabelStrip` (two visible labels plus existing `+N` overflow behavior). Do not introduce a separate agent/LLM classification for the stack unless later product evidence shows the union semantics are insufficient. The group-level label presentation should mean “this conversation contains messages with these labels”, not “the stack itself was independently classified”.

- Reply-to-sender regression identified after the closed client-fix stage. The imported email data is intact: Gmail/Yandex normalizers persist `metadata.sender`, `metadata.headers["reply-to"]` when present, recipients, subject, message-id/thread metadata. The regression is at the Assistant visibility/routing boundary: `assistant/tool_output.py::_bounded_object()` intentionally strips arbitrary object metadata, so the model can read an email body while not seeing its canonical sender envelope. This must NOT be fixed by re-exposing arbitrary metadata or weakening the untrusted-data/prompt-injection invariant.
- Architecture decision for reply parity: use **reply by exact Object** across communication providers. Existing Mattermost/Telegram/Teams `send_message(reply_to_object_id=...)` remains canonical and server-resolved. Email must gain symmetric object-anchored reply preparation: the model supplies the exact email `reply_to_object_id` plus reply body; backend resolves and freezes recipient using RFC envelope semantics (`Reply-To` first, otherwise `From`), source provider/account, reply subject, and threading headers before creating the approval plan. Never infer a recipient from body/signature text. Compose-new-email remains supported separately.
- Email source-account provenance gap: newly materialized Gmail/Yandex email objects should record their connected source account identity in metadata (no schema migration). Legacy objects should resolve the source account deterministically from connected account vs To/Cc when possible, or a sole eligible account; genuine ambiguity must fail/clarify rather than guess. Selectively expose bounded normalized email sender/reply-to identity in Assistant read output for informational questions, but not provider routing secrets/arbitrary metadata.
- Telegram MTProto AI activation/quarantine policy is unchanged by this work. No provider gate may be relaxed as part of reply parity.


- Architect clarification/correction on prompt-injection handling: **semantic source data must not be hidden merely as an injection defense**. An injection can appear in any source field (body, subject, sender display name, address-like text, filename, label, metadata, URL, etc.), so field stripping is not the security boundary. The universal invariant is control/data separation: every field originating from stored/external/UI/tool object data is untrusted evidence/data only and never an instruction, regardless of how imperative it looks. Only actual system/developer/user instruction channels define intent and authorization. Assistant-visible object projections should preserve normalized user-relevant semantic fields broadly. Exclusions are only for genuine secrets/credentials/auth material, non-user-facing internal sensitive state, or bounded size/cost concerns; those exclusions must not be described as prompt-injection protection.
- Exact-object routing remains the mutation safety boundary: for replies, the model may inspect source metadata but should anchor the action to the exact communication Object; backend deterministically resolves/freeze the canonical route. Thus source content cannot redirect the action even if it contains attacker-controlled addresses/instructions. Approval/action-plan rules remain unchanged.
- Exact-object reply parity implemented at `036e34db44024ffd1fa536409bbc1a75a1ffb98f`. Email reply uses `send_email(reply_to_object_id, body)`; the backend freezes recipient from Reply-To or else From, source account, `Re:` subject, In-Reply-To/References, and Gmail thread id. New Gmail/Yandex objects store `source_account_email` without a migration. Assistant object output keeps bounded semantic envelope fields and hides credential-like metadata. No live provider send. - Graph Refined P3 Person salience foundation implemented at `3402cb2f450a036efc6ce4a6415441b43947a31e`. Salience is derived at read time for existing active People and their active exact identities. Components are directness, reciprocity, capped frequency, recency decay, capped public exposure, P2 route-choice/confirmation attention, and an existing task or calendar edge. A noisy public author stays below a less frequent direct reciprocal contact. Unknown senders do not create People. Rejected, deleted, and cross-user People contribute nothing. The result records that salience does not decide object importance. - Graph Refined P3R salience attribution and noise-starvation safety implemented at `52928587b8ff4f5586b216e8ffb989cc28a50c3d`. Gmail and Yandex direction come from SENT/INBOX or sent/inbox folder metadata; an inbound recipient is not treated as an outbound interaction, and ambiguous mail does not create reciprocity. Multi-recipient or CC mail is not 1:1. The read scans up to 200 recent messages in the 90-day window and keeps direct and public hits under separate per-Person caps, so newer unrelated traffic does not erase a direct contact. Ranking uses People who have interaction, attention, or a task/calendar link, not the oldest created People. Teams outbound and application senders fail closed. Mattermost direct and public inbound stay exact-identity only. - Graph Refined P3R3 salience candidate dedupe and scoring consistency implemented at `34d96e4b66b4eb92aceb8b0aebac96d635fd9c5c`. Attention and task/calendar candidate bounds count distinct People. Each Person is represented by its strongest newest feedback or newest relevant edge. Mind map keeps earlier nodes still when a chain grows by one leaf, and moves the whole chain when context is attached at the root or the chain direction reverses. Radial moves almost the whole field for all three changes. Force moves the whole field when a node or context edge is added, and does not move it when only edge direction reverses. - Architect visual direction after human V2R review: KEEP CURRENT GRAPH AS THE PRODUCT MAP CANDIDATE; EVALUATE LIBRARIES ONLY AS GEOMETRY HELPERS. Human comparison on real data found the existing current renderer materially easier to understand than V1 GraphView and V2 ELK because it preserves a stable global geography and already supports selection-centered dimming/focus. The next research question is no longer which library should own the whole graph layout. Secretary keeps semantic scene selection, global positions, focus lens, semantic zoom, and Flutter card rendering. Candidate libraries may only refine local geometry: collision avoidance, small local displacement, and edge routing while preserving the current map. Evaluation sequence: V3A Graphviz local post-processing on Linux, then V3B fCoSE against the same geometry-refinement contract and fixtures. Only if Graphviz materially wins should Android/NDK packaging be researched; only if both candidates miss a concrete criterion should another engine be added. Islands/Areas remain deferred until focus-lens navigation is evaluated on the stable map.

- Architect final acceptance of Visual Task Map V3B: ACCEPTED AS NEGATIVE PRODUCT RESULT / fCoSE NOT SUITABLE AS DEFAULT LOCAL COLLISION REFINER. Accepted implementation SHA `e1ad6de211b350c97a1eff1ed3ff53da763ee3fd`; Executor HOLD `1fba8b56a0dfc8646b7940d6fc7924d0f7f91f03`. Review confirms the implementation stayed within the intended architecture: current `GraphLayout` remains the global map/default renderer; V1 GraphView and V2 ELK remain optional experiments; `fcose` 0.1.0 is isolated to the adapter; `GraphGeometryScene / GraphGeometryRefiner / GraphGeometryResult` is engine-neutral and allows optional routes; no backend/ontology/Areas/edge-router/deploy changes. Fixed-node constraints work well: selected/fixed obstacles had 0.00 px drift in all probes. However the product target fails on ordinary flower geometry: Preserve moves 15/15 movable nodes with max displacement 488.42 px, turns 0 overlaps into 12 and straight-edge intersections 2 into 10; Relax moves 15/15 with max displacement 497.09 px, turns 0 overlaps into 7 and intersections 2 into 9. The forced overlap fixture is repaired, proving the engine can separate a collision, but it does so by force-layout relaxation rather than by preserving the recognizable current flower. One-leaf stability is also poor on the flower (15 pre-existing movable nodes move). Conclusion: fCoSE is useful evidence that fixed constraints are feasible, but it is not accepted as Secretary's normal local collision helper. Keep the engine-neutral refinement contract; do not tune fCoSE further unless a later concrete need emerges. The next candidate should solve local rectangle collision with explicit attraction to original positions rather than perform graph layout.

- Architect visualization research checkpoint after V1/V2/V3A/V3B: CURRENT CUSTOM GRAPH IS THE LEADING PRODUCT-MAP FOUNDATION; NEXT STEP IS FOCUS LOD / COMPACT FLOW SATELLITES. The encrypted architect recovery context was refreshed to `SECRETARY-ARCHITECT-CONTEXT-v42` at commit `4323f178599999a408a5f4811954ccd17102c45f`; strategy supplement remains unchanged. Research conclusions are now explicit: GraphView V1 did not improve real-data comprehension and its layouts were unstable; ELK V2 produced excellent collision/routing geometry but visually replaced the useful stable geography and moved all measured nodes after a one-leaf change; Graphviz remains unevaluated because the Executor host lacks `neato` and installation requires interactive sudo, so it is not rejected but should not consume more automated cycles; fCoSE V3B proved fixed-node constraints (0 px fixed drift) and repaired a forced overlap, but normal flower geometry became materially worse (all movable neighbors moved, new overlaps/intersections appeared), so it is not accepted as the default local collision helper. Keep the engine-neutral geometry-refinement contract, but stop searching generic full-graph layouts by default. Product concept now under test: Tasks remain stable full-card geographic anchors; selected Task keeps its full Flow flower; non-selected Tasks keep their Task cards while associated Flow is rendered as compact type/provider/bookmark satellites/halos with no title/date text; moving focus collapses the old flower and expands the new one without moving global Task positions. Initial ordering, if needed, must use explainable signals (bookmark, recency, deterministic tie-break), not opaque importance. Individual satellites must be capped with deterministic overflow aggregation. This is presentation/LOD only; no ontology, Area, hierarchy, backend, or Task semantic change. Human review of this spike should decide whether the map direction is right before more library research or selected-flower collision/routing work.
- Visual Task Map V4 Focus LOD compact Flow satellites implemented at `76ed9fbd600f0dd1994d050bd0c16aa0788d53dd`. No new dependency. The current `GraphLayout` remains the global map and the startup renderer. V1 GraphView, V2 ELK, and V3B fCoSE stay available. People mode stays on the current renderer. Tasks mode can switch to `Фокус LOD` without reloading workspace data and without running ELK or fCoSE. Task top-left coordinates stay the controller `GraphLayout` positions. Selecting another Task does not recompute them. Satellite diameter is 24 px. Placement is local concentric rings around the anchor Task card: 12 slots, 4 px gap, first radius the corner clearance of the 186×100 card inflated by half a satellite plus the gap, then a 28 px step. The ring does not reuse the Flow card's old rectangle and does not move Tasks. Cap is 20 individual satellites plus one `+N`. Choice order is bookmark, then existing `updatedAt` descending, then object id. A compactable Flow connected to several visible Tasks is drawn once, beside the Task whose current center is closest, with Task id as the tie-break. That anchor is presentation-only and is not written into edges or workspace data. Compact kinds are email, calendar_event, event, file, document, dataset, folder, note, web_page, chat, message, and chat_message. Task, person, project, label, scheduled_activity, temporal_hint, and unknown kinds stay full cards. Edges to compact satellites are not painted. Task-to-Task edges and the selected Task's full Flow edges stay. A satellite click selects the anchor Task. Overview fixture A: 5 Tasks, 0 full Flow cards, 8 satellites, overflow 0, Task overlaps 0, same-halo overlaps 0, duplicate Flow visuals 0. Selected flower B: 2 Tasks, 10 full Flow cards, 4 satellites, Task drift 0 px, Task overlaps 0, same-halo overlaps 0, duplicate Flow visuals 0. Shared Flow C renders once on the nearer Task and becomes a full card when the farther Task is selected. High-count halo D: 20 satellites and `+15`, Task overlaps 0, same-halo overlaps 0. Close Tasks E: 8 satellites, Task overlaps 0, same-halo overlaps 0, max ring radius 127.4 px. A second close pair with 8 satellites on each Task also had 0 cross-halo overlaps. Density comparison for the selected flower: the current renderer draws 14 full non-Task cards; Focus LOD draws 10 full non-Task cards plus 4 satellites. Compact halos are tight on the first ring in these fixtures. The placer clears Task rectangles and satellites in the same halo, and does not reserve a neighboring halo, so a denser pair can still meet. `+N` does not show the hidden titles until that Task is selected. A compact Flow connected to several Tasks draws no long edge to the non-anchor Task. A selected Task with many direct Flow cards stays a full flower; that density is not solved here. - Architect ontology/presentation decision while V4 is in progress: DO NOT INTRODUCE A SEPARATE DIRECTION OBJECT KIND. Keep one canonical `task` concept with an explicit completion/closure mode distinguishing finite/completable work from ongoing/continuous activity. Absence of `due_at` alone MUST NOT imply ongoing: finite Tasks may have no deadline, and ongoing activity may have a planning horizon. Human-facing presentation may call an ongoing Task a “направление” or “деятельность”. Visual grammar should deliberately distinguish it from finite work: ongoing Tasks are candidate cluster anchors, rendered as a round/circular or strongly rounded node, with an infinity/continuous-process glyph and somewhat stronger visual emphasis; finite Tasks keep the current task-card/check semantics. Meaning must not rely on color alone. Composition invariant under consideration: ongoing may contain ongoing or finite Tasks; finite may contain finite Tasks; ongoing MUST NOT be a compositional child of finite work because a finite parent cannot complete while a continuous child is definitionally non-terminal. This is distinct from `depends_on`. Flow/evidence should remain directly attached to the relevant Task/ongoing Task and should not gain its own workload hierarchy. Do not implement this while V4 Focus LOD is running; treat it as the next ontology/presentation refinement after human review of V4.

- Architect final acceptance of Visual Task Map V4: ACCEPTED TECHNICAL SPIKE / FOCUS LOD IS THE LEADING PRESENTATION DIRECTION / HUMAN REAL-DATA VISUAL REVIEW REQUIRED. Review confirms the intended architecture: current `GraphLayout` remains the stable global geography and startup renderer; V1 GraphView, V2 ELK, and V3B fCoSE remain separate experiments; Focus LOD is a renderer-only projection over unchanged loaded workspace objects/edges; Task coordinates are not recomputed or persisted; no new dependency/backend/schema/ontology change. Compact Flow is represented once, presentation anchoring is deterministic and non-semantic, selected-Task Flow remains full-card context, non-selected Task Flow becomes 24 px satellites, per-halo individual satellites are capped at 20 plus `+N`, compact Flow long edges are suppressed only in this renderer, and switching focus preserves Task drift at 0 px. The spike materially reduces full-card density while keeping current map semantics. Known non-blocking limitations for human review: halos reserve Task rectangles and same-halo satellites but not neighboring halos globally; high-density neighboring halos may still collide; overflow hides titles until expansion; multi-Task compact Flow does not visualize the non-anchor long relation; a selected Task with many direct Flow objects remains a dense full flower. Do not optimize these yet. HOLD until real-data screenshots compare Current vs Focus LOD; only after that decide whether to refine halo geometry, address selected-flower density, or proceed to explicit finite/ongoing Task presentation.

Preserve the earlier metrics as valid evidence of instability, but do not treat them as a product rejection. Revised invariant: stable Task anchors matter most; Flow/context presentation nodes may move to obtain better packing. Next research spike should combine LOD and fCoSE in one presentation scene: canonical Task positions remain fixed; fCoSE may position/refine both full expanded Flow cards and compact Flow glyphs using their actual presentation sizes; LOD decides large vs compact representation. Measure Task drift (must remain 0), Flow displacement, overlaps/cross-halo collisions, focus-change stability, and readability. Do not yet add finite/ongoing Task presentation or far-horizon semantic zoom; those follow after the hybrid near-field renderer is visually accepted.

- Task completion mode V7A implemented at `ae476abad7ff00b90c23c3cc4769dfd9d15d2a08`. Alembic revision `0050` revises `0049`. Ongoing may be `open`, `in_progress`, `cancelled`, `archived`, or deleted through the existing delete path. Finite status behavior is unchanged. Capture, ObjectCreate, and notification acceptance persist `finite` unless a Task create/patch explicitly supplies `ongoing`. Selecting an ongoing Task keeps its direct Flow compact. Selecting a finite Task still opens the V6 local medium-card flower. Canonical Task center drift across those focus changes is 0 px. - Visual Task Map V7B dandelion geometry implemented at `0b76ffb463a18ec1deaf3fab56c0fc7d6e6eadaa`. No new dependency, no new renderer, and no backend/schema change. `fcose` stays 0.1.0. The current renderer stays the default. V1–V4 and V7A finite/ongoing behavior stay. Only `LOD+fCoSE` compact and focused starts change. Hybrid halos are allocated in anchor-id order. Within a halo, bookmark, then `updatedAt` descending, then id assigns marks to deterministic slots. A halo that fits in one nominal ring (12 compact slots or 8 focused slots) is spread across the full circle. A larger halo fills nominal slots per ring and staggers the next ring by half of that nominal slot, so adjacent rings do not reuse the same ray. Compact marks are 32×32 px. Finite anchors use the 186×100 rect. Ongoing anchors use the 144×144 circle's bounding rect and the same canonical center. Ring radius is the larger of the anchor clearance and the radius that separates marks by their diagonal plus gap. A blocked preferred angle is searched on the same ring first, then on later staggered rings. Tasks do not move. Overflow `+N` joins the same ring plan. After those starts, Preserve and Relax still run. A focused fCoSE pass is kept only when the existing locality limit holds, overlaps do not worsen, every focused card stays within 22.5° of its own start ray, and every pair of focused rays stays at least 11.25° apart. A compact pass is kept only when no mark is missing, overlaps do not worsen, every mark stays within 30° of its own start ray, halos of at least 4 marks cover at least 3 quadrants, and halos of at least 6 marks cover 4 quadrants. A failed guard reverts that entire pass to the balanced starts. Isolated compact starts: 2 marks at 180° (2 quadrants), 3 at 120° (3 quadrants), 4 at 90° (4 quadrants), 6 at 60° (4 quadrants). Ongoing 8-mark halo covers 4 quadrants and hairlines meet the circle. Compact 21 marks use rings of 12 and 9 with a 15° phase offset and minimum separation 15°. Two halos of 8 marks whose tasks are 260 px apart reserve space with 0 raw overlaps and stay repeatable. A 4-card flower is at 90°. A 12-card flower uses rings of 8 and 4 with minimum separation 22.5°. A focused flower beside an ongoing circle does not overlap the 144×144 rect. Task center drift is 0 px. On these fixtures Preserve and Relax tie: each accepts 3 compact passes and 1 focused pass, and each falls back on the 21-mark halo and the 12-card flower. Presentation bounds for the accepted small compact halo are about 357×271 Preserve and 433×347 Relax; the fallback 12-card flower is 780×850. A partial outer ring keeps the nominal slot, so its unused arc is larger than an equal full-circle gap. Dense flowers that fail the angular guard stay on the balanced starts instead of the fCoSE positions. Combined graph, Task Profile, V1–V6, V7A ongoing, and V7B tests: 120 passed, 3 failed. - Architect final acceptance of Visual Task Map V7B: ACCEPTED TECHNICAL GEOMETRY PASS / HUMAN REAL-DATA REVIEW REQUIRED BEFORE HIERARCHY. Accepted implementation `0b76ffb463a18ec1deaf3fab56c0fc7d6e6eadaa`; Executor HOLD `9e907624516a7c4c1d2dc3b0c81d859340c76ce1`. Review confirms V7B stays presentation-only and preserves V7A semantics: finite and ongoing Tasks keep canonical centers with 0 px drift; compact hybrid marks use actual 32×32 geometry; ongoing anchors use the actual 144×144 presentation obstacle; focused medium cards remain 156×76. The new balanced allocator is count-aware for one-ring cases, uses nominal 12-slot compact / 8-slot focused rings for larger cases, staggers subsequent rings by a half-slot, reserves previously placed halos deterministically, and searches alternate angles/rings without moving Tasks. fCoSE remains a helper behind all-or-pass topology guards: focused passes must preserve locality, non-worsening overlaps, <=22.5° own-ray drift, and >=11.25° pair separation; compact passes must preserve non-worsening overlaps, <=30° own-ray drift, and quadrant coverage. Dense 21-mark compact and 12-card focused fixtures correctly fall back to balanced starts when fCoSE breaks topology; smaller fixtures are accepted. This is considered desirable behavior, not a failure. Preserve and Relax are currently tied on the deterministic fixtures, so do not choose one as final yet. Do not begin `part_of` until human screenshots confirm that real compact halos now read as dandelions around both rectangular finite Tasks and circular ongoing anchors, and that selected finite flowers no longer show repeated radial axes.

- Human real-data visual review of V7B: DANDELION / LOCAL-FLOW GEOMETRY IS VISUALLY ACCEPTED. The overview now reads as stable Task anchors with compact Flow halos rather than a generic field of cards; small halos visibly occupy multiple directions around their anchor, and selected finite Tasks open into localized medium-card flowers without the earlier long rays or obvious repeated radial stacking. Remaining defects are polish-level (some obstacle-driven asymmetry and occasional longer spokes), not a reason to reopen the architecture. This is enough to treat the near-field LOD + fCoSE + balanced-start model as the working product-map base. One V7A visual proof is still missing in the supplied screenshots: no Task has yet been visibly switched to `completion_mode=ongoing`, so the 144×144 circular infinity/Direction anchor has not been human-reviewed on real data. Before starting `part_of`, manually switch an appropriate existing Task such as “Публикации в научной прессе” through the normal editor (“Редактировать” -> “Направление” -> “Сохранить”) in the development database and inspect the same cluster. Do not hardcode or migrate that user data. If the ongoing center reads correctly, proceed to Task composition/hierarchy as the next conceptual stage; do not do more dandelion algorithm work first unless the ongoing screenshot exposes a concrete geometry problem.

- Human final visual judgement for the current near-field map: ACCEPTED AS GOOD ENOUGH TO MOVE FORWARD; REMAINING DANDELION WORK IS POLISH, NOT A BLOCKER. Human reports that the balanced compact halos are materially better and the overall visualization now looks very good. Remaining desired polish is presentation-level: compact Flow glyphs may become slightly larger, and collapsed Flow should feel a little more visibly grouped around its anchor Task/ongoing center. Do not spend another architecture cycle on this now. Keep these as later visual-polish items while preserving the current stable Task geography, balanced halo starts, fCoSE topology guards, and finite/ongoing presentation semantics. The next conceptual stage may proceed to Task composition/hierarchy (`part_of`), but only through a separately assigned CURRENT_TASK; HOLD remains until that task is written.

- Architect map-edge/productization decision after V7B real-data acceptance: CANONICAL RELATION SEMANTICS STAY RICH; THE PRIMARY TASK MAP MUST NOT VISUALIZE RAW EDGE DIRECTION AS A UNIVERSAL ARROW. Existing operational relations such as `depends_on`, `waiting_on`, `delegated_to`, `requested_by`, `involves`, and `references` remain canonical because they serve Task Profile/operational truth, even when they are not continuously drawn on the map. Presentation rule: arrows encode only intrinsically directional semantics, never “which node happens to be visually central”. Task↔Flow evidence/context is an attachment and should use a non-directional line/hairline. Generic Task↔Task links (`related_to`, legacy `contains`, similar contextual links) use non-directional structural lines in the map. `depends_on` is a directional operational cross-link and may use an arrow from dependent Task to dependency, preferably only when one endpoint is in focus so overview geography stays clean. Task↔Person role edges, label/temporal edges, and Flow↔Flow source-local relations should not clutter the primary Tasks overview; their semantics remain available in details/Task Profile/People UI. Future `part_of` is not implemented yet; when introduced, canonical orientation should be child/source -> parent/target, which naturally points rootward if an arrow is ever shown, but normal overview may still use a plain structural line. Next task is productization before hierarchy: promote the accepted LOD+fCoSE hybrid to the sole Tasks renderer in the visible UI, remove experimental renderer selectors/warnings, keep Preserve/Relax for now, and introduce edge-presentation classification so generic raw source->target arrows disappear. Also reserve compact halo angles around visible Task↔Task structural rays so a thin Flow hairline cannot lie directly on top of a major Task edge as seen in the human screenshot. Do not implement `part_of` in the same task.

- Architect correction to V8A edge policy after human review: SEMANTIC OBSERVABILITY OVERRIDES THE PREVIOUS “MOST MAP EDGES NON-DIRECTIONAL” SIMPLIFICATION. If Secretary reasons over a canonical relation type and its source/target orientation, the human must be able to inspect the same type and direction. The primary map may still use LOD and hide classes that are irrelevant to Tasks overview, but any relation it does render must preserve its semantic directedness. Never reverse an arrow merely to point toward a visual center/root. Direction comes only from canonical relation semantics. Initial Tasks-map grammar: `related_to` is symmetric/plain line; `references` is directed source->target (Task evidence therefore Task->Flow) with a light arrow; `depends_on` is directed dependent/source -> prerequisite/target with a dashed arrow; legacy `contains` remains directed according to its actual source->target semantics and must not be silently reinterpreted as future `part_of`; future `part_of` convention remains child/source -> parent/target. Compact LOD may simplify geometry but must not create a contradictory direction: compact Task->Flow hairlines should retain a small low-emphasis arrowhead or otherwise expose the direction directly at the mark. The selected-object detail panel must act as a semantic audit view and show relation type plus canonical source -> target (or an undirected separator for symmetric types), together with provenance/state where available, so the human can see exactly what Secretary sees. Different relation classes should have a minimal consistent line vocabulary (solid/dashed/weight/arrowhead) without exploding into many visual encodings. Actor-role/label/temporal/source-local edges may remain absent from the Tasks canvas if they would clutter it, but their relation rows/detail semantics must remain available and directionally explicit. This correction supersedes the immediately prior V8A map-edge simplification note where generic directed relations were to lose arrowheads.
- Visual Task Map V8A-R implemented at `afb602c13fc8a9e0ea882663b1ee8ae09185dc2e`. No new dependency and no backend/schema change. Tasks mode no longer shows `Текущий`, `Эксперимент`, `ELK`, `fCoSE`, `Фокус LOD`, or `LOD+fCoSE`. The Tasks canvas renders the accepted hybrid LOD+fCoSE presentation directly. People mode is unchanged. Preserve and Relax stay visible in Tasks mode; the default remains Preserve. Ordinary topology fallback stays on the presentation as `topologyNote` and is not shown as a product banner. A genuine refinement failure still shows `graph-hybrid-fallback`. Map grammar: `related_to` is a solid line with no arrow; `references` is a light solid line with the arrow at the canonical target, and a compact Task->Flow hairline uses a small arrow at the Flow when that edge is the chosen anchor; `depends_on` is a dashed secondary line with the arrow at the target; legacy `contains` stays a solid arrow from its actual source to its target. Future `part_of` is documented only as child/source -> parent/target and is not implemented. Actor, label, temporal, and Flow↔Flow edges stay off the Tasks canvas and remain in the detail row. A proposed directed edge keeps the tertiary proposal color and its semantic arrow; dash means `depends_on`, not proposal. The relation row reads `Source —[type]→ Target` for directed edges and `Source — Target` for `related_to`, with the selected end labeled `Этот объект`, plus origin and state. - Architect final acceptance of Visual Task Map V8A-R: ACCEPTED. Implementation `afb602c13fc8a9e0ea882663b1ee8ae09185dc2e`; Executor HOLD `f3552945ff1d189789332f174821cfa52eb9288c`. - Architect decision for the next composition stage: introduce canonical `part_of` as Task->Task composition with orientation CHILD/SOURCE -> PARENT/TARGET. The composition graph should be a forest, not a general DAG: each active Task has at most one active non-rejected `part_of` parent; a parent may have many children; self-links and cycles are invalid. Cross-cutting membership should use `related_to`, labels, or other relations rather than multiple composition parents. Completion-mode compatibility: finite child -> finite parent allowed; finite child -> ongoing parent allowed; ongoing child -> ongoing parent allowed; ongoing child -> finite parent forbidden. Changing completion mode must not be allowed to create an invalid existing composition. Legacy `contains` is NOT migrated or reinterpreted. No automatic status cascade, parent completion rule, time inheritance, or operational-state change in the first `part_of` stage. First stage should implement canonical semantics/API/profile/tool/UI observability and map arrow only; global hierarchical repositioning follows in a separate visual stage after semantic proof.

- Human visual-polish note before/alongside hierarchy work: the accepted hybrid near-field map is good enough, but global cluster spacing now exposes legacy geometry assumptions. Because Task centers were originally laid out for much larger visible evidence cards, collapsed dandelion clusters are now spaced too far apart; several cluster centers also fall into a mostly vertical column despite substantial free horizontal space. This is future GLOBAL MAP PACKING polish, not a reason to reopen near-field Flow geometry. Desired direction: keep semantic/stable geography but allow a later hierarchy-aware/global compaction pass to use screen area more evenly and shorten distances between cluster centers. Do not solve this inside V8B1 semantic part_of.
- Human UI-polish note: the right object-detail pane should not permanently consume roughly a quarter of the graph viewport while nothing is selected. Desired behavior: when no object is selected, the pane is collapsed/absent and the graph uses the full width; selecting an object slides/opens the detail pane; clearing selection closes it again. This is a separate presentation task after current composition semantics unless it becomes necessary for hierarchy review.
- Task composition V8B1 implemented at `d19edc6a6b76436ea9a8216c499d6647bdda36c9`. Canonical relation is `part_of`, oriented child/source -> parent/target. One edge occupies the parent slot; a second parent->child edge is not stored. The graph is a forest: zero or one non-rejected parent, any number of children, self-parent invalid, cycles invalid. A proposed `part_of` occupies the same slot. A second parent is rejected until the existing relation is removed; nothing is silently reparented or auto-rejected. Allowed completion pairs are finite->finite, finite->ongoing, and ongoing->ongoing. Ongoing child -> finite parent is rejected on create, proposal, and confirmation. Changing `completion_mode` is rejected when the task still has an active finite parent or an active ongoing child that would break that matrix. `GraphService.create_edge` and confirmation through `set_edge_state` share `validate_part_of_edge`; the edge being confirmed is ignored so it is not counted as a second parent. Reject stays available. User REST and `RelationService` can create a confirmed `part_of`. `link_objects` uses the same write: proposed in agent mode, confirmed in approved-confirmed mode, and its description states child -> parent, both endpoints Tasks, one parent, composition rather than dependency. Task Profile adds `parent_task`, `child_tasks`, and `child_tasks_truncated`. `depends_on` and `dependent_tasks` stay separate. The client shows `Входит в` and `Состав`; a click re-roots through the existing Task navigation. The audit row stays `Child —[входит в]→ Parent`, including when the parent is selected. The Tasks map draws `part_of` as a solid structural arrow from child to parent, stronger than `references` and not dashed like `depends_on`. Positions are unchanged. Legacy `contains` was not migrated, reversed, or copied into `part_of`; any future cleanup needs human review. `part_of` does not block parent completion, auto-complete, inherit status or dates, change operational state, change Today inclusion, or propagate archive/delete. No hierarchy-aware layout was started. Focused composition tests passed, including the matrix, non-task/self/second-parent/cycle rejection, relation-API idempotency, proposed slot occupancy, confirmation revalidation, completion-mode guards, user create, `link_objects`, and profile separation from dependency. Relation API, domain-tool, completion-mode, operational, correlation-decision, and removable-relation tests passed. Finite circumradius is half the diagonal of 186×100. Ongoing circumradius is 72, from the 144×144 circle centered on the same Task center. Sibling sectors start at -π and cover 2π; width is proportional to subtree node count. Sibling order is the task-only `GraphLayout` polar angle around the parent, then id. Each component envelope is the presentation rects inflated by 80 px on every side. Those envelopes are shelf-packed by area descending, then component key: row width is sqrt(total envelope area) × 1.4, and the gap after inflation is 24 px. Preserve/Relax does not move Tasks. Fixture D: depth 2, grandchildren farther out and inside the parent sector, bounds 1179×896, 0 overlaps. Fixture E: ongoing root clearance 72+80+finite circumradius, bounds 423×144. Fixture G: a `depends_on` between two trees does not change sectors; the two envelopes still stacked as 2 rows × 1 column because each is wider than the leftover shelf. - Task Map V8E1 confirmed `part_of` workspace closure implemented at `f9c074be21f08af4a80381325215f9c4b83a68f8`. Backend Graph workspace only. No schema, client, People workspace, or production change. For a Task root, and for overview seeds, a breadth-first walk follows confirmed Task↔Task `part_of` in both directions before ordinary one-hop expansion. The frontier starts in root or seed order. Incident confirmed edges are taken in edge-id order. Each Task and edge is added once. `neighbor_limit` does not cap this walk; `node_limit` does. Confirmed composition edges are omitted from the later ordinary neighbor ranking so they do not consume `neighbor_limit` slots. Remaining node budget is then spent on the existing one-hop expansion from the original root or original `seed_ids` only. Hierarchy-added Tasks do not become new Flow centers. `seed_ids` and seed ordering are unchanged. A non-Task root stays one hop. Proposed `part_of` does not walk the hierarchy; a direct proposed neighbor can still appear through the existing one-hop, and a further proposed hop does not. Rejected `part_of` adds nothing. Overflow past `node_limit` sets `truncated=true` and does not exceed the limit. Repeated calls keep the same node and edge id order. Client code did not change, so Linux debug was not rebuilt. New closure tests passed. Workspace, active-seed, caps, and composition tests: 44 passed, 1 failed. The failure is `test_rooted_deleted_task_can_be_inspected`, which also fails on the unmodified V8E1 parent service: a deleted Task root is already hidden from active reads and returns 404. Ruff check/format passed. `git diff --check` clean. Production remains `296b4735f9473ea60ef22f1827ed94260603128e`, Alembic `0047 / 0047`. Relation repair and V8E2 were not started.

- Architect technical review of Task Map V8E1: ACCEPTED. Implementation `f9c074be21f08af4a80381325215f9c4b83a68f8`; Executor HOLD `9d29ef3272b7dbaa3ed01a5531f4e362070c379b`. Review confirms confirmed Task↔Task `part_of` closure is breadth-first, bidirectional, deterministic by original root/seed order plus edge id, bounded by the existing global `node_limit`, and not capped by `neighbor_limit`. Closure runs before ordinary one-hop expansion and hierarchy-added Tasks do not become additional Flow expansion centers. Confirmed `part_of` is excluded from the later generic neighbor ranking so it does not consume ordinary neighbor slots. Proposed `part_of` can still appear as an ordinary direct neighbor but does not recursively close; rejected `part_of` does not expand. API/schema/client/People workspace/production are unchanged.
- The sole reported V8E1 suite failure, `test_rooted_deleted_task_can_be_inspected`, is a pre-existing stale test contract rather than a V8E1 regression. - Architect review of People P1R2: INCONCLUSIVE / VERIFIER DEFECT FOUND. Implementation `aa263f5947af3e5cd736ab54e60c29fd27b23868`; Executor HOLD `6682355de579df2cc399fba7d33205032b6c65a2`. The one-shot production authorization was consumed after `CENSUS_MARKER=started`; no rerun is authorized from P1R2. Static review identifies a concrete two-layer output-contract defect that can explain the observed `malformed` result without any production-data anomaly. Remote `run_census()` feeds `BEGIN TRANSACTION READ ONLY; ... SELECT ...; COMMIT;` to `psql -At` and then requires exactly one non-empty stdout line. Normal psql command tags can yield `BEGIN`, the data row, and `COMMIT`, causing the remote helper to emit `CENSUS_BLOCKED=census_output` even when the aggregate SELECT succeeded. The local `parse_remote_output()` only recognizes a blocked response when it is the sole line; after the consumed marker, a valid two-line `CENSUS_MARKER=started\nCENSUS_BLOCKED=...` response is itself reported as `malformed`. The six production counts are not recoverable from repository state because the remote helper captured/discarded the raw psql stdout instead of forwarding it. No production conclusion may be inferred.
- Architect next step: local-only P1R2R verifier repair/rehearsal. It must reproduce the psql command-tag behavior on a disposable/local PostgreSQL instance or deterministic subprocess fixture, fix both the remote psql-output boundary and the local consumed-blocked parser contract, and add regression tests. P1R2R must not connect to production, SSH, production HTTP, production DB, or providers. A second production census, if ever desired, requires a NEW explicit Architect authorization after P1R2R review.
- People P1R2R census verifier repair implemented at `38d71caded818b79398f7b5848d0d6ec07c1c2d6`. Root cause reproduced: local psql 18.6 against disposable Postgres 16 with the old flags `-v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -At` printed `BEGIN`, the data row, and `COMMIT`, which the old one-line parser rejected. The same script with `-X -q -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -At` printed only the data row. The helper now accepts exactly those two shapes and still requires the row to start with read-only `on`. A post-marker `CENSUS_BLOCKED=<known stage>` stays that stage with the authorization consumed, instead of `malformed`. Disposable rehearsal on synthetic rows returned `3|2|1|2|1|1` and the local parser accepted the protocol. `classify()` still covers both decision branches on synthetic fixtures. Tests: `python3 -m unittest ops/production/tests/test_people_p1_person_census.py` — 10 passed, 1 skipped without the rehearsal flag; the rehearsal test itself passed in the same cycle before the container was removed. `git diff --check` clean. Production, SSH, production HTTP, production DB, and providers were not used. Verifier status: `READY_FOR_NEW_AUTHORIZATION`. No second census was run. Production remains `296b4735f9473ea60ef22f1827ed94260603128e`, Alembic `0047 / 0047`.

- Architect review of People P1R2R: ACCEPTED. Implementation `38d71caded818b79398f7b5848d0d6ec07c1c2d6`; Executor HOLD `1a80e47fb6c8e28b910ab644e47a24544ec65a6e`. The P1R2 failure mechanism was reproduced locally against disposable Postgres: the old `psql -At` protocol emitted command tags `BEGIN`, the aggregate row, and `COMMIT`, which the one-line remote parser rejected; the local parser then misclassified a post-marker known blocked stage as generic `malformed`. The repaired remote invocation uses `-X -q -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -At`, accepts only the exact single-row form or the explicitly enumerated legacy `BEGIN/data/COMMIT` form, and still requires read-only state `on`. The local parser now preserves known post-marker blocked stages with `consumed=true`. Disposable rehearsal returned the expected six synthetic integers; focused verifier tests passed. Production/SSH/providers were untouched. Verifier status: `READY_FOR_NEW_AUTHORIZATION`.

- Architect cold-start follow-up after C1: explicit creation alone does not yet activate a source-derived identity. A bare Person can receive a grounded candidate from stored communication by name/evidence matching, but Graph UI `confirm` currently writes only `user_confirmed` evidence. `PersonAssistantService._effective_keys` and canonical communication matching use active `PersonIdentity` rows, so a confirmed candidate without P4 orchestration is not yet a canonical exact identity for communication retrieval. The next bounded step is C2: on an explicit grounded first-party confirmation, attach the exact identity canonically when it is unowned, while preserving conflict safety, idempotency, reject/retract behavior, and the distinction between a pre-existing identity and one created by this confirmation. Do not create Person↔Flow edges or run background/provider/model enrichment.
- People C2 explicit confirmation activation implemented at `a283302208139111ccb2138897d4153495ba5c0d`. `POST /graph/people/{person_id}/identity-correction` with `action=confirm` still records `user_confirmed` evidence under `graph_ui:` / `user_feedback`. When the exact identity is not already attached, that confirmation calls `PersonIdentityService.attach` once and stores the new `PersonIdentity` id on the confirmation row. A repeated confirm keeps one active identity and one active confirmation row. An identity already attached to the same Person is not duplicated, and its confirmation row stays unlinked so retract does not detach it. Retract of a confirmation whose `person_identity_id` points at the active identity created by this confirmation rejects that identity; retract of a rejection does not. Reject after activation suppresses the identity through the ledger and leaves the row stored, and retracting that rejection restores it. Another owner's identity and an existing confirmation conflict still fail closed with no new identity or evidence. No edge, schema change, provider call, model call, or `PersonEnrichmentService.plan()` call was added. Client code did not change. Backend: `tests/test_person_c2_activation.py`, `tests/test_person_graph_workspace.py`, `tests/test_person_identity.py`, `tests/test_person_evidence_ledger.py`, `tests/test_person_assistant.py`, `tests/test_person_enrichment.py` — 88 passed. - Architect Task stabilization S1 diagnosis: both remaining lifecycle failures share one contract drift between active-read visibility and mutation/delete lookup. Active reads intentionally hide both canonical tombstones (`deleted_at != NULL`) and legacy `status="deleted"` Tasks. Mutation paths currently call `GraphService.get_object()`, so same-user legacy-deleted Tasks become generic not-found before the Task-specific `deleted task cannot be modified` guard can run. Separately, `TaskMutationService.soft_delete_task` delegates legacy `status="deleted", deleted_at=NULL` rows to `ObjectDeletionService`, which only treats an existing tombstone as already deleted, so a repeated semantic delete is reported as `changed=true`. S1 should unify Task mutation/delete semantics across both deletion forms without changing active-read visibility, generic object deletion, restoration, schema, or production.
- Task Stabilization S1 deleted-task mutation/delete convergence implemented at `d95d7086c4b9113fd8896e370308f3f52464a2be`. `TaskMutationService.load_task_for_mutation` loads by object id and user id without active-read filtering, requires `kind=task`, and when `allow_deleted` is false rejects either a canonical tombstone or legacy `status=deleted` with `deleted task cannot be modified`. Field patch, status mutation, and the model-facing `update_task` preflight use that helper. `DomainToolService._get_task_for_mutation` delegates to it. Tool and direct PATCH/status on both deleted forms return that same message and do not change fields, status, edges, or evidence. First delete of an active Task still returns `changed=true`, writes `deleted_at`, sets status `deleted`, keeps the row and incident edges, and enqueues the existing embedding side effect. A repeated delete of a canonical tombstone returns `changed=false` and keeps the original `deleted_at`. A delete of legacy `status=deleted` with `deleted_at=NULL` returns `changed=false` and leaves `deleted_at=NULL`. Tool and REST delete agree. A no-op delete does not enqueue embedding. `GraphService.get_object` still hides both deleted forms, and a done Task stays visible. Generic non-Task deletion was not changed. `tests/test_task_s1_deleted.py` plus `tests/test_task_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_direct_tasks_api.py`, `tests/test_universal_object_delete.py`, `tests/test_graph_workspace.py`, `tests/test_graph_workspace_part_of_closure.py`, `tests/test_h2a_completion_mode.py`, `tests/test_h2b_link_objects.py`, `tests/test_mcp.py`, `tests/test_domain_tools.py`, and `tests/test_tool_gateway.py`: 151 passed, 0 failed. The former `test_update_task_deleted_task_rejected` and `test_delete_task_idempotent` failures are green. Ruff check passed; touched Python was formatted. `git diff --check` clean. - Task Stabilization S2 manual create finite/ongoing parity implemented at `60c5157f8781dad82b555bf3b1642dd30267f430`. `POST /capture/task` `completion_mode` is optional: omitted stores finite, explicit `finite` stores finite, explicit `ongoing` stores ongoing with `status=open`, explicit null is rejected with `completion_mode must be finite or ongoing`, and any other string is rejected. A `SegmentedButton` with key `capture_task_completion_mode` sits above the text field: `Задача` is finite, `Направление` is ongoing, and the default is `Задача` / finite. It is disabled while capture inputs are disabled or submitting. `CaptureDraft` defaults to finite, `copyWith` keeps the mode unless changed, `toRequest()` sends `completion_mode`, and `CaptureDraft.empty` resets to finite. `setCompletionMode` updates only the mode and clears a non-submitting error the same way text and title edits do. `mergeDraft`, context attachment, dependency attachment, a failed submit, and voice text edits keep the selected mode. - Human Direction visual gate attempt after S2: BLOCKED BY ENVIRONMENT VERSION SKEW, not yet a verdict on S2 behavior. Screenshot/observation showed Task edit failing with Pydantic `extra_forbidden` for body field `completion_mode=ongoing`, manual Capture accepting the UI selection but producing an ordinary finite Task, and Task detail failing to load the Task profile. Static ref comparison explains all three symptoms exactly: current `main` `TaskPatchRequest` accepts/validates `completion_mode`, current `CaptureTaskRequest` accepts finite/ongoing and forwards it, and current tasks router exposes `GET /tasks/{task_id}/profile`; production ref `296b4735f9473ea60ef22f1827ed94260603128e` has none of those additions. Its Task PATCH schema has `extra="forbid"` and no `completion_mode`, so edit rejects the field; its Capture schema lacks `completion_mode` and does not forbid extras, so a newer client field is silently ignored and the server creates the default finite Task; its task router has no profile endpoint. Therefore the observed behavior is consistent with a current-main/newer client connected to the old production backend. Re-run the human gate only against current-main backend with current DB schema (Alembic 0050) and current-main client. Production remains untouched. Direction remains canonical `kind=task` + `completion_mode=ongoing`, not a separate Object kind; human presentation/renderer should distinguish it by mode.

- Executor process refinement after the S2 Direction gate: manual GUI/visual validation is now human-only by default. The user observed the Executor eventually exercising the local current-main UI successfully enough to create multiple ongoing Directions, create relations among them, create a finite Task and convert it to ongoing, after an earlier connection/auth setup failure. Treat this as informal exploratory evidence that the current-main create/edit/relation path can work locally, but not as the formal human visual-quality verdict. The Executor spent excessive time on UI interaction that the user can perform much faster. Going forward, Executor tasks must stop before manual GUI clicking unless explicitly authorized; automated tests/build/backend readiness remain Executor work, while layout/UX/screenshots are handed to the user.

- Architect rollout decision after S2: proceed toward production/preprod validation on real data, but not through the normal schema-neutral deploy harness. Production is still runtime/ref `296b4735f9473ea60ef22f1827ed94260603128e` at Alembic `0047`, while current main requires the additive migration chain `0048 -> 0049 -> 0050`. `ops/production/deploy.py` intentionally rejects migration-bearing releases. Therefore the next production step is D1: implement/rehearse a dedicated exact `0047 -> 0050` migration deployment harness with explicit pre-cutover rollback and post-cutover fail-closed rollback guards. No production mutation is authorized in D1 itself.
- D1 migration semantics to preserve: 0048 adds `person_identities`; 0049 adds `person_identity_evidence`; 0050 adds nullable `objects.completion_mode`, backfills existing Tasks to `finite`, and constrains values to finite/ongoing. Pre-release-runtime failure may safely downgrade back to 0047 because newly added tables/column have not yet been used by release code. After release runtime starts, automatic downgrade must be blocked unless it is directly proven safe: no Person identity rows, no Person evidence rows, and no Task has `completion_mode='ongoing'` (and no other release-created state would be destructively lost). If safety cannot be proven, keep application stopped or preserve 0050 schema and emit a break-glass marker rather than dropping user data.
- Production D1 exact `0047 -> 0050` migration harness prepared at `48d482d1bac211e11272dc8a526fbcc547c870ec`. Local entrypoint `ops/production/migrate_people_tasks_0050.py`; remote helper `ops/production/remote_migrate_people_tasks_0050.py`. The release SHA is an argument. The only accepted rollback is `296b4735f9473ea60ef22f1827ed94260603128e`, and the only accepted revision pair is `0047 -> 0050`. Against that rollback, current main's Alembic delta is exactly the added files `0048_person_identities.py`, `0049_person_identity_evidence.py`, and `0050_task_completion_mode.py`, with chain `0047 -> 0048 -> 0049 -> 0050` and unchanged Alembic env, config, template, and earlier revisions. The remote order checks out the release, builds `api` and `worker` while the previous containers keep serving, stops only those services, runs `alembic upgrade 0050` with `--rm --no-deps`, verifies revision `0050` and the structural counts, then recreates only `api` and `worker`. `db` is never recreated and `.env` is never rewritten. Before release runtime starts, failure may downgrade to `0047` and restore the rollback application. After release runtime starts, downgrade proceeds only when `person_identities`, `person_identity_evidence`, and Tasks with `completion_mode='ongoing'` are all proven zero; otherwise the harness leaves `api` and `worker` stopped, does not downgrade or delete rows, and emits `BREAK_GLASS_REQUIRED=true`. A rerun already at the release SHA and Alembic `0050` verifies invariants and does not migrate again. Inconsistent checkout/revision pairs fail closed. Disposable PostgreSQL rehearsal: schema initialized at `0047` with one Task and one non-Task; upgrade to `0050` stored the Task as `finite`, left the non-Task NULL, and created both Person tables empty; downgrade returned to `0047` with those tables and `objects.completion_mode` gone. A later `ongoing` Task made the harness refuse downgrade and left the revision at `0050`. The rehearsal container was removed. Tests: `backend/tests/test_people_tasks_0050_migration_harness.py` 25 passed; together with `backend/tests/test_assistant_0047_migration_harness.py` and `backend/tests/test_production_migration_deploy.py`, 82 passed. Ruff check passed and the new Python was formatted. `git diff --check` clean. No SSH, no production connection, and `origin/production` was not moved. It remains `296b4735f9473ea60ef22f1827ed94260603128e`, Alembic `0047 / 0047`. Client files are unchanged since `60c5157f8781dad82b555bf3b1642dd30267f430`, which already had a successful Linux debug build. This harness does not install a client; a separate client update is still required on the production workstation. D2 was not started.

- Architect review of Production D1: ACCEPTED. Implementation `48d482d1bac211e11272dc8a526fbcc547c870ec`; HOLD/main `fd45df20ff53ad973f22e461ff84f3cb5c251b8a`. The dedicated `0047 -> 0050` migration harness is accepted: exact rollback SHA and revision pair are fixed, Alembic delta is restricted to added migrations 0048/0049/0050 with unchanged prior migration infrastructure, build occurs before downtime, only api/worker stop/recreate, DB container/volume/.env are preserved, structural checks run before release runtime starts, pre-cutover rollback may downgrade to 0047, and post-cutover downgrade is fail-closed unless Person identity/evidence tables are empty and no ongoing Task exists. - Human Direction visual gate after Production D2 / Client C0: PARTIAL PASS WITH CORRECTIVE REQUIRED. Against the real production backend, the human successfully created an ongoing Direction through Capture. The same gate exposed a finite -> ongoing edit defect on the existing Task titled “Академ”: the editor reported success, but the Graph continued to present the object as a finite rectangular Task and reopening/visible properties still presented it as Task. This is not accepted as a completed finite/ongoing edit path. Code review confirms the backend Task PATCH contract and TaskMutationService support completion_mode and existing unit/widget coverage proves the outbound payload in isolation, so the corrective must reproduce the full human path through edit -> mutation callback -> Graph presentation/readback rather than merely add another request-shape test. Code review also found that client object summary text derives “Задача” from kind=task unconditionally; since ongoing Directions intentionally remain canonical kind=task, human-facing summary/presentation must use completion_mode to say “Направление” without changing ontology. The human also requested the legacy full-screen Capture form be brought into visual/field parity with the newer Task editor. Architect decision: authorize S2R as one bounded corrective: make finite <-> ongoing mutation visibly truthful and stable, then converge manual Capture onto the same Task/Direction field model (mode, title, description, due date, planned interval) while preserving context/dependency/voice/drop behavior. Extending POST /capture/task with optional due/planned fields is application-contract work only; no schema migration is required because the object columns already exist. No production deploy is authorized in S2R. The previously observed SECRETARY_API_BASE_URL precedence issue remains separate and is not part of this corrective.
- Task/Direction S2R implemented at `f2469610e188072b7695b4dc43426e018a645f65`. Root cause of the human finite -> ongoing failure was client presentation and success feedback, not a missing PATCH field: the editor already sent `completion_mode`, and production Task PATCH already persisted it. Graph and detail summaries still labeled every `kind=task` as `Задача`, the rectangular node used that same kind label, ongoing circles were drawn only when the hybrid layout object existed, and the snackbar always said `Задача обновлена` even when the returned object did not match the requested mode. S2R maps human Task/Direction labels from `completion_mode`, draws `HybridOngoingTaskNode` for every ongoing Task, and shows mode success only when the mutation response reflects the requested mode. Manual Capture now uses the shared mode, title, description, due-date, and planned-interval controls; the action is `Создать задачу` or `Создать направление`; reset returns finite with no due date and no planned interval; a failed submit keeps those fields. `POST /capture/task` accepts optional `due_at`, `planned_start_at`, and `planned_end_at` with the existing both-or-neither planned-interval rule. No migration. Backend `tests/test_capture_s2_completion_mode.py`, `tests/test_direct_tasks_api.py`, and `tests/test_task_completion_mode.py`: 31 passed. Client `test/capture/capture_test.dart`, `test/graph/task_direction_edit_test.dart`, and `test/api/secretary_api_client_test.dart` passed; `test/tasks/task_management_actions_test.dart` kept the two pre-existing delete-action failures. Flutter analyze of touched files: 0 errors, with the pre-existing Capture drop-target warning and pre-existing Graph infos. `flutter build linux --debug` succeeded. `git diff --check` clean. Production was not deployed. A later schema-neutral backend rollout is required for the new Capture fields, and a client refresh is required for the presentation fix. S3 and H2D were not started.

- Architect review of Task/Direction S2R: ACCEPTED. Implementation `f2469610e188072b7695b4dc43426e018a645f65`; HOLD/main `5a55885ca302c38df22f98a263372a89beceb7bc`. The change is application-only with no Alembic/schema delta. The finite <-> ongoing persistence contract was already present; S2R correctly fixes human-facing Task/Direction labels/presentation and response-verified success feedback, converges manual Capture with the editor field model, and extends Capture with optional due/planned fields using existing object columns. Production remains `fd45df20ff53ad973f22e461ff84f3cb5c251b8a`; S2R is not deployed. Architect decision: do not spend a separate production rollout on S2R yet. Human testing has exposed a second application-only Graph relation-stability defect; implement that corrective first, then perform one combined schema-neutral backend rollout and client refresh for S2R + Graph corrective before the next human gate.
- Human Graph/tree gate observation after D2: when modeling a real Task/Direction tree, manual `part_of` creation can appear successful and remain visible across an immediate revisit, then disappear from the canvas after Secretary attaches another related object; a previously visible set of five publication/evidence neighbors was also observed as four. This is not yet evidence of DB data loss. Current code review shows direct user relation creation writes a confirmed edge and successful request sessions commit, while Graph workspace is bounded (default seed 12, neighbor 12, node 80), advertises truncation, and ranks ordinary neighbor candidates by random UUID edge id. Confirmed `part_of` closure is prioritized for initial root/seeds, but a Task admitted later as an ordinary neighbor does not recursively receive the same structural closure. The selected-object detail pane also derives its relation list from workspace edges, so a relation can disappear from the visible relation inventory merely because one endpoint was omitted from the current canvas. Existing `GET /objects/{id}/neighbors` returns direct relation truth independently of Graph workspace projection. Architect decision: next corrective must separate persistence truth from bounded canvas projection, make structural `part_of` visibility stable, remove UUID-only neighbor membership behavior, and add relation-type visual previews using the already accepted V8A-R edge grammar.
- Graph G1 implemented at `057e5a649f89b97d41bfa6158f6477dddd0e4592`. The human symptom matches bounded projection, not deletion of confirmed user edges: the canvas and the old detail list only draw relations whose endpoints are inside the workspace, ordinary membership was UUID-ordered, and a Task admitted as an ordinary neighbor did not close its confirmed `part_of` component before later optional neighbors. Ordinary candidates are now ordered by `Edge.created_at` ASC, `Edge.id` ASC. After each newly admitted Task, its confirmed `part_of` component is closed before the next ordinary neighbor is admitted. `node_limit` still caps the workspace and sets `truncated` when the remainder does not fit. The selected object's relation section loads `GET /objects/{id}/neighbors`, shows a direct relation even when the other endpoint is not drawn, marks it `не на карте`, and centers that object by its id. A failed neighbors read keeps the canvas and shows a relation-section error with retry. The truncation banner says the map is only part of the graph, hidden relations may still exist, and the selected object's full direct list is in the detail pane. The relation chooser previews each of the four types with `presentGraphMapEdge`: `Связано с` is an undirected solid line, `Ссылается на` is a light directed solid arrow, `Зависит от` is a dashed directed arrow, and `Входит в` is a structural solid arrow from child to parent. No schema change and no new relation type. Backend graph workspace/caps/part_of/person suites: 45 passed, 2 failed. Those two failures, `test_overview_seed_includes_depth_two_descendants_that_are_not_seeds` and `test_user_isolation_keeps_other_user_hierarchy_out`, also fail on the unmodified service against this workstation database because the bootstrap user already has other open tasks. Focused `tests/test_graph_workspace_g1.py`: 3 passed. Client graph workspace, controller, map relation, part_of, proposed relation, hybrid, and direct-inventory tests: 68 passed, 3 failed. The three failures open object detail through `Подробнее` and then look for `Удалить` or `Спросить секретаря`; the delete case fails the same way on the unmodified graph screen. Flutter analyze of the touched graph files: 0 errors, 6 pre-existing infos. `flutter build linux --debug` succeeded at `client/build/linux/x64/debug/bundle/personal_secretary`. `git diff --check` clean. Production was not deployed and remains `fd45df20ff53ad973f22e461ff84f3cb5c251b8a`, Alembic `0050 / 0050`. S2R and G1 are both schema-neutral and can be rolled out together later. S3 and H2D were not started.

Review confirms the reported symptom is addressed as bounded-projection instability rather than invented DB deletion: ordinary neighbors now use monotonic `created_at ASC, id ASC`; Tasks admitted through ordinary expansion close confirmed `part_of` before later optional neighbors within the existing node bound; the selected-object direct relation inventory is sourced independently through `GET /objects/{id}/neighbors`; truncation UX explains canvas-vs-persistence; and relation chooser previews are driven by the accepted map edge presentation grammar.
