- Human G1 post-R1 gate: G1 persistence/projection split is confirmed, but a narrower workspace edge-completeness defect remains. On exact R1/G1 client, selected ongoing Task/Direction “Создание курсов” shows direct-neighbor truth from `GET /objects/{id}/neighbors`: confirmed user `part_of` from “Курс по траблшутингу” -> “Создание курсов”, confirmed user `part_of` from “Создание курсов” -> “Основная работа”, plus a direct `related_to` to “Основная работа”. The other endpoints are visibly present on the current canvas; “Основная работа” is not marked `не на карте`. Nevertheless the corresponding structural lines are absent from the map. Therefore the relations are persisted and both endpoints are admitted, but the workspace/canvas edge set is incomplete. Code review indicates GraphWorkspaceService accumulates edges encountered during structural closure / ordinary admission and then only filters that accumulated map by endpoints; it does not perform a final persisted-edge closure over the final admitted node set. Architect decision: authorize G1R to make workspace edge membership complete for persisted active/non-rejected relations whose endpoints are already admitted, while keeping node bounds unchanged. This is application-only; no schema migration and no production deploy in G1R.

- Additional human G1R evidence before Executor start: with “Создание курсов” selected, pressing `Показать связи` immediately made the missing structural arrows appear, including the visible chain through “Курс по траблшутингу”, “Создание курсов”, and “Основная работа”. Code review confirms this button calls `GraphWorkspaceController.expandSelected()`, which fetches a rooted `GET /graph/workspace?root_id=<selected>` and merges that response into the already loaded overview; it is not a pure visibility toggle and does not write relations. Therefore the renderer/LOD can draw the edges once present, and the stronger reproduction is: overview admits the Task endpoints but omits persisted edges; rooted workspace for the selected Task returns those edges; client merge then makes them visible. G1R should target overview/rooted workspace edge-set consistency/final edge closure, not painter changes.
- Graph G1R implemented at `f3ac86fc66087c4310b9d90e3fa10d1a730e9392`. Root cause: after the bounded node set is final, `GraphWorkspaceService` only kept edges the admission walk had already inserted. A persisted non-rejected edge between two admitted nodes was omitted when that walk never selected it. Before the fix, a fresh-user overview admitted a seed and two neighbors but omitted `related_to` between the neighbors, while the rooted workspace of one neighbor returned that same edge. Confirmed `part_of` among three admitted Tasks was already returned by G1 closure. A second relation between the same admitted endpoints was not: `part_of` was present and the parallel `related_to` was absent until rooted merge. The fix runs one query after the final node set, for the current user only, and merges every non-rejected edge whose source and target are both already admitted. It does not add nodes. Rejected edges stay out. Another user's edge between the same objects stays out. Proposed edges stay in. `node_limit`, `neighbor_limit`, seed selection, G1 `created_at` ordering, and structural closure are unchanged. No painter or LOD change. No schema change. Backend: `tests/test_graph_workspace_g1r.py` 6 passed; together with `tests/test_graph_workspace_g1.py`, `tests/test_graph_workspace_part_of_closure.py`, `tests/test_graph_workspace.py`, `tests/test_graph_workspace_caps.py`, and `tests/test_relations_api.py` — 50 passed, 2 failed. Those two remain `test_overview_seed_includes_depth_two_descendants_that_are_not_seeds` and `test_user_isolation_keeps_other_user_hierarchy_out` on this workstation's bootstrap user. Client graph workspace, controller, map relation, part_of, hybrid/focus LOD, direct inventory, and the new G1R widget test: 54 passed, 3 failed. The three failures remain `Details delete refreshes overview without deleted task`, `Details delete current root falls back to overview`, and `Details Ask Secretary does not refresh disposed Graph screen`. Flutter analyze of `test/graph/graph_workspace_g1r_test.dart`: no issues. `flutter build linux --debug` succeeded at `client/build/linux/x64/debug/bundle/personal_secretary`. Ruff check passed. `git diff --check` clean. Production was not deployed and remains `2a765d68ca8c7bc859e4902f00df6bb2d3b9bc6b`, Alembic `0050 / 0050`. G1R adds no migration, so a later rollout can be schema-neutral from that production SHA through the normal deploy harness. S3 and H2D were not started.

- Architect review of Graph G1R: ACCEPTED. Implementation `f3ac86fc66087c4310b9d90e3fa10d1a730e9392`; HOLD/main `fccc2b4c1c01430721865bd118f7eb2bbd207b38`. Confirmed root cause is traversal-dependent workspace edge accumulation: overview could admit both endpoints but omit an eligible persisted edge, while rooted workspace returned the same edge and `Показать связи` merged it so the existing renderer drew it. G1R adds one bounded post-admission edge-closure query for the current user, includes every non-rejected persisted edge whose endpoints are already admitted, preserves proposed edges, adds no nodes, preserves node/neighbor limits and G1 ordering/part_of closure, and does not touch painter/LOD. Focused G1R backend regressions pass, including parallel `part_of` + `related_to` between the same endpoints; client regression confirms the existing renderer/controller behavior once the edge is present. No Alembic/schema change. Client product code did not change; only a client test was added, so the exact R1 client already in human use does not require refresh for this corrective. Authorize one schema-neutral backend application rollout from production `2a765d68ca8c7bc859e4902f00df6bb2d3b9bc6b` to exact release `fccc2b4c1c01430721865bd118f7eb2bbd207b38`, Alembic remaining `0050`. Human must then repeat the overview test BEFORE pressing `Показать связи`.

An already admitted shared Flow does not spend another slot, and G1R closure keeps every persisted edge among admitted nodes. A Task brought in by `part_of` receives the same priority pass. A rooted non-Task does not expand that Task's other Flow evidence. `node_limit` still caps admission and sets `truncated` when priority Flow remains outside. `neighbor_limit` still applies only to ordinary neighbors. - Human Graph architecture correction after R3: do NOT expose raw `node_limit` as the next product solution. The core UX defect is semantic inconsistency from node-wise truncation: a user cannot trust a visible region if arbitrary nodes/edges from the same Task hierarchy or evidence cluster may have been silently removed. New product invariant: overview is a bounded **semantic window**, not a bounded arbitrary node sample. A visible Task constellation/direction must be complete as a unit; when the graph is larger than one window, whole constellations move in/out together. Navigation should eventually behave like a lens over a larger surface: panning toward an edge loads adjacent semantic windows and evicts distant ones, rather than accumulating the whole graph. Architect stages this as G3A (whole-constellation windows + deterministic adjacent window navigation and observability) followed by G3B (wire the same next/previous window primitive to InteractiveViewer pan thresholds with stable geography). Raw Node Limit profile setting is deferred/cancelled as the primary fix.
- Graph G3A implemented at `d96daa800b0d596e37d6fd24817845a7075d7970`. Overview membership is no longer seed nodes plus arbitrary admission until `node_limit`. A constellation is the complete active current-user confirmed `part_of` tree of its topmost active Task, plus every G2 priority Flow on those Tasks (`confirmed`, origin `user` or `agent`, type `references` / `related_to` / `depends_on`, the other endpoint an active non-Task). A standalone Task is a one-Task constellation. Nested Directions in the same top-level tree are one page. A constellation is an overview page only when it contains at least one non-terminal Task, so a done child stays inside an open parent and a done-only tree stays off the default overview. Order is the minimum seed key among non-terminal members: missing `due_at` last, then earliest `due_at`, confirmed before other states, most recently updated, then Task id. Whole constellations are packed in that order. `SOFT_WINDOW_NODE_TARGET` is 80 and is not a cut inside a constellation: a non-empty window that would cross 80 puts the next constellation on the next page, and the first constellation is kept whole even above 80. Shared Flow counts once inside a window. `MAX_COMPLETE_WINDOW_NODES` is 500. One constellation above 500 raises `ConstellationTooLargeError` (`constellation is too large for complete overview rendering`, HTTP 422) and is never sliced. The client says a focused view is required and does not build that view. `GET /graph/workspace` accepts `window_index` (default 0). An out-of-range index is HTTP 422, not a clamp. Successful Tasks overview adds `window_index`, `window_count`, `has_previous_window`, `has_next_window`, `constellation_root_ids`, and `semantic_window_complete=true`. `truncated=true` on that overview means other semantic windows exist. Source/system neighbors do not decide overview fit. A rooted Task loads its whole constellation and G2 evidence even when ordinary context is truncated; ordinary neighbors cannot evict constellation members. A rooted non-Task keeps the existing bounded path and does not pull every touched Task tree. The client replaces overview state on `loadOverviewWindow` / next / previous, clears selection when the object leaves the window, and shows previous/next only when more than one window exists, with tooltips `Предыдущая область графа` and `Следующая область графа` and status `Область N из M`. Returning to overview restores window 0. Preserve/Relax do not change page membership. Direct inventory can still mark an off-window object `не на карте`. No schema change. Backend graph suites: 75 passed, 2 failed; the two remain `test_overview_seed_includes_depth_two_descendants_that_are_not_seeds` and `test_user_isolation_keeps_other_user_hierarchy_out` on this workstation's bootstrap user. Ruff check passed. Client semantic-window, controller, workspace, screen, G1R, map relation, inventory, part_of, and LOD coverage passed except the three pre-existing detail-route failures (`Удалить` / `Спросить секретаря`). Flutter analyze of touched client files: 0 errors, 8 pre-existing infos. `flutter build linux --debug` succeeded. `git diff --check` clean. Production was not deployed and remains `6a38a22303c0af882fcb32c4a7ac91b708cb52f4`, Alembic `0050 / 0050`. G3A adds no migration, so a later rollout can be schema-neutral from that production SHA. G3B, S3, and H2D were not started.

Review confirms the Tasks overview membership model is now semantic-window based rather than arbitrary node-wise truncation. A constellation is the full active current-user confirmed `part_of` tree at its topmost Task plus G2 confirmed user/agent `references|related_to|depends_on` Flow evidence for every Task in that tree. Overview includes a constellation only when it contains at least one non-terminal Task; terminal descendants remain with an open ancestor. Whole constellations are ordered deterministically by the minimum non-terminal Task seed key (missing due last, earliest due, confirmed first, newest update, Task id), then packed into windows with soft target 80 without splitting a constellation. A single constellation may exceed 80 and remains whole; above emergency ceiling 500 the service fails explicitly rather than slicing it. Overview `window_index` metadata is additive; `truncated` now means other semantic windows exist, not an incomplete visible constellation. - Human G3A production gate: PASS. On the exact R4 client, the ordinary overview no longer showed the old incomplete-graph warning and visually presented the expected Task/Direction constellations with compact Flow evidence. A completed publication Task (“ICDM”) is intentionally absent from the default overview because its constellation has no open Task, reducing background noise. Selecting its open parent Direction and pressing `Показать связи` brings the completed Task back as local/rooted context; selecting that completed Task and pressing `Показать связи` then reveals its Flow evidence. This matches the intended split: overview contains trustworthy active semantic windows, while completed/history context remains explicitly reachable. Remaining UX defect: `Показать связи` is currently one-way; controller `expandSelected()` fetches a rooted workspace and merges it into the current state with no inverse. Desired follow-up is a reversible local-context affordance (`Показать связи` <-> `Скрыть связи`) that removes only the corresponding locally expanded context and restores the prior semantic-window state without changing persistence. Treat this as a small post-G3A corrective; G3B pan/lens behavior remains a separate later stage.

- Human post-G3A layout evidence: creating a structural Task/Direction relation between existing constellations can leave stale geometry. Example: attaching “Публикации в научной прессе” under Direction “Академ” moved the Direction anchor but existing Task cards stayed at their prior positions, producing long rays across the map. Switching app tabs and pressing fit-to-view did not repair it; a full fresh overview/restart is expected to. Code review confirms the cause: relation creation calls `mergeRelationContext()`, which stages the new edge and then `_mergeWorkspace(...)`; both call `GraphLayout.computePositions(... existing: _positions, freshRoot: false)`. In `GraphLayout._layoutOverview`, when existing positions are non-empty and `freshRoot=false`, only newcomers receive positions and the method returns, so already positioned nodes are intentionally not recomputed after topology changes. Fit-to-view changes only the transform and cannot repair stale coordinates. Desired corrective: distinguish structural Task<->Task topology mutation (at minimum `part_of`) from ordinary Task<->Flow evidence addition. After structural mutation, refresh the authoritative semantic overview/rooted constellation and perform a fresh layout of the affected semantic area (bounded whole-window relayout is acceptable); do not preserve stale Task coordinates merely for geography stability. Ordinary Flow satellite/evidence writes may keep incremental layout. Also ensure semantic-window membership/paging metadata is refreshed when `part_of` merges or splits constellations.
- Graph G3A-R1 implemented at `dc7bde42cc1a382c911fb968f35518f69356193c`. Confirmed client root cause: after a Task↔Task write, `mergeRelationContext` / `removeEdge` / `upsertEdge` kept the previous coordinates because `GraphLayout.computePositions` with `freshRoot: false` treats existing positions as fixed geography and only places newcomers. Fit-to-view changes only the camera. The topology predicate is Tasks mode and a map-visible Task↔Task relation: both endpoints `kind=task` and the type is outside `kGraphMapHiddenRelationTypes`, which covers `part_of`, `related_to`, `references`, and `depends_on`. `part_of` is treated as topology-invalidating in Tasks mode even when an endpoint kind is not loaded. Create, user delete, and proposal confirm/reject of such a relation do not finish on the incremental path. They replace the current overview window or rooted workspace from the server, recompute positions with fresh layout, request one fit, and keep Preserve/Relax unchanged. Overview refresh uses the current `windowIndex`, replaces membership, keeps selection only if the object is still present, and copies `window_index`, `window_count`, previous/next flags, and `constellation_root_ids`. If that window is out of range, the controller loads window 0 once and does not loop. A rooted refresh keeps the same root and selects it when it still exists. Task↔Flow and other non-topology writes stay on incremental merge and do not move already positioned Task anchors. If the relation write succeeded and the follow-up workspace refresh fails, persistence is not rolled back and is not reported as a write failure; coordinates are cleared, load state is error, and the message is `Связь сохранена, но граф не удалось обновить. Обновите обзор.` Retry is the existing overview reload, with no automatic network loop. The corrective is client-only and schema-neutral. Backend product code did not change. `graph_workspace_controller_test.dart`: 26 passed. `graph_part_of_dialog_test.dart`, `graph_map_relation_test.dart`, and `graph_direct_relation_inventory_test.dart`: 13 passed. `graph_workspace_g1r_test.dart`, `focus_lod_test.dart`, `graph_proposed_relation_test.dart`, and `task_map_hierarchy_test.dart` passed in the same run as the dialog failure that was then fixed. Semantic-window and hybrid/focus LOD tests passed. `graph_workspace_screen_test.dart` failed only the three pre-existing detail-route finders for `Удалить` and `Спросить секретаря`. Flutter analyze of the touched client files: 0 errors, 8 pre-existing infos. `flutter build linux --debug` succeeded at `client/build/linux/x64/debug/bundle/personal_secretary`. `git diff --check` clean. Production was not deployed and remains `489741540e30a775e2ea086f3976d7305512afe2`, Alembic `0050 / 0050`. `Скрыть связи`, G3B, S3, and H2D were not started. A later rollout needs a new client build; the backend SHA does not need to move.

- Architect review of Graph G3A-R1: ACCEPTED. Implementation `dc7bde42cc1a382c911fb968f35518f69356193c`; HOLD/main `dbc3aa55ddff4b3ccb13668977b7908cccc3513b`. Diff review confirms product changes are client-only: Task-map topology invalidation is explicit at controller level, not a global layout behavior change. In Tasks mode, map-visible Task<->Task `part_of|related_to|references|depends_on` mutations refresh the authoritative current overview/rooted workspace, replace state, recompute fresh geometry, refresh G3A semantic-window metadata, and request one fit; `part_of` is fail-safe topology-invalidating even when an endpoint kind is not currently loaded. Task<->Flow evidence remains incremental and preserves existing Task anchors. Create/delete/proposal decisions share the same topology-refresh path. If a write succeeds but refresh fails, persistence is not rolled back or misreported; stale coordinates are cleared and a recoverable graph-refresh error is shown. No backend/schema change; production remains `489741540e30a775e2ea086f3976d7305512afe2`, Alembic `0050`. Human validation requires a new exact-client build; backend production need not move.
- Ontology alignment audit checkpoint: the current core remains conceptually `Person / Task / Flow / Time`. Task/Flow/Time are well aligned across canonical backend contracts, Task Profile, first-party UX, internal Assistant tools, and derived operational state. People/Identity is a strong identity/route foundation but not yet a social/organizational ontology: current People workspace intentionally forbids organizational edge types such as `manager_of`, `works_with`, `colleague`, `role_at`, and `member_of`; roles such as “director”, “manager”, and “colleague” therefore require a separate ontology design pass rather than ad-hoc Person<->Person edges. A concrete parity gap also exists: internal Assistant `create_task/update_task` exposes Task actor/dependency semantics, while the hand-written MCP wrappers currently omit those actor/dependency parameters and Person-specific Assistant tools are not MCP-exposed. These are backlog parity/design items, not part of G3A-R1.
- Product decision: add a very small ontology orientation near the top of the Secretary system instructions in the next bounded prompt corrective, rather than relying only on scattered procedural rules. Canonical compact wording to test/implement: `Core ontology: Person=who; Task=commitment/Direction; Flow=evidence/context; Time=when. Relations are explicit facts, never inferred.` This is a conceptual orientation, not a claim that Flow or Time are literal Object kinds, and it does not authorize unsupported inference or mutations. A second concise actor-role reminder may be evaluated separately. Existing Assistant turn telemetry already records tool calls, Responses rounds, and token usage, so prompt impact can be regression-tested/benchmarked without architectural changes.

- Human C1 / G3A-R1 gate: FAIL, with a narrower second-layer layout cause. Removing the structural relation caused the old publication “daisy” to gather correctly, proving the G3A-R1 authoritative refresh + fresh controller relayout path is active. Re-adding the relation scattered the map again with long rays. Code review localizes this to `projectTaskMapHierarchy`, which is applied by the screen after controller positions. It builds confirmed `part_of` trees as hierarchy components, removes all hierarchy members from `freeIds`, and computes free connected components only from non-`part_of` edges among those remaining free ids. Therefore a visible non-`part_of` Task<->Task edge crossing from a hierarchy member to an otherwise-free Task is ignored for component packing even though the edge remains rendered. Adding `part_of` can thus move the Direction into a hierarchy component while its former related/referenced/dependent Task “petals” are shelf-packed elsewhere, producing deterministic long rays. Deleting `part_of` returns them to one ordinary connected component and gathers them. This is not stale controller geometry and not a persistence defect.
- Corrected presentation invariant for the next client corrective: structural parentage and visual packing are orthogonal. Confirmed `part_of` alone determines parent/child hierarchy and radial depth. All map-visible Task<->Task edges (`part_of|related_to|references|depends_on`) determine visual packing connectivity. A cross-link must never become parentage, but it must prevent directly connected Task clusters from being arbitrarily shelf-packed far apart. In particular, attaching an existing Direction to a parent Direction must move/pack its existing visible Task daisy with it rather than leave the petals behind. No relation semantics or persistence should change.
- Graph G3A-R2 implemented at `703a0a12ca236c328744cdbc2994ebc40e71ff42`. Confirmed client root cause: after G3A-R1 refreshes coordinates, the screen still applies `projectTaskMapHierarchy`. That projector built confirmed `part_of` trees, removed those Tasks from the free set, and packed remaining free components only by non-`part_of` edges among free ids. - Graph G3A-R3 implemented at `754e5ef2b0a9049939d77836d2341b3138d38406`. Local relation context is view state only. Each successful `Показать связи` records the selected object id once, in activation order, as an explicit expansion anchor. A node already in the base can still be an anchor. Repeated Show does not duplicate the anchor. A failed Show does not mark it active. Selection, focus, and camera changes do not clear anchors. `Скрыть связи` removes only that anchor, then reads the authoritative base before changing the canvas: the current semantic window when `rootId` is null, or the current rooted workspace otherwise. An out-of-range overview window recovers once to window 0. A missing or deleted rooted base uses the existing overview fallback. Remaining anchors are fetched in activation order and merged with the existing incremental layout. Shared nodes stay when another active replay still returns them. A replay anchor that is gone or deleted is dropped. The rebuild does not re-root. After success the selected object stays selected when it still exists, a missing selection or edge is cleared, and one fit is requested. G3A window metadata after an overview rebuild comes from the base overview response. Preserve/Relax is untouched. Anchors clear when the base is replaced by mode switch, overview load, window navigation, re-root, missing-root fallback, ordinary refresh, or a G3A-R1 Task-to-Task topology refresh. A failed Hide keeps the visible graph and the previous anchors and shows `Не удалось скрыть связи. Обновите обзор и повторите.` People mode uses the same read path and restores the People base. - People R3 implemented at `27ba03bddc04c4a1cb0875db39efc32a079a1fc0`. `POST /graph/people/{person_id}/emails` with `{"email":"..."}` binds one exact email to an existing active Person. `normalize_email` produces identity type `email`, provider `email`, empty realm, and the case-folded canonical value. A malformed address returns 422 and writes nothing. If no active Person owns the address, the existing identity service attaches it and records one `user_confirmed` evidence row with provenance `graph_ui:manual_email:{canonical}` and explanation `explicit manual email binding`. When that call creates the PersonIdentity row, the evidence links to it. Repeating the same binding returns the existing confirmation and does not add another identity or confirmation row. Ownership by another active Person, an active confirmation on another Person, or an identity row that cannot be attached returns 409 `person email is already bound` and does not reassign or merge. `POST /graph/people/{person_id}/identity-correction` still rejects a confirm that was not already exposed as a candidate. A mistaken binding is rejected and restored through the existing correction actions; rejection hides it from effective contacts and from exact-identity Flow attribution, and retraction restores both. A stored email becomes attributable through the existing direction rules without a Person↔Flow edge. Another user receives 404 and no write. Rooted Person detail shows «Добавить email» only when the rooted salience summary is present. The dialog asks for one address, explains that only an exact confirmed address is added, and keeps malformed or conflict errors in the dialog. Success refreshes the rooted Person so the contact and R2 Flow projection update. Overview selection does not show the control. No migration, provider lookup, Telegram/Teams/Mattermost binding, merge, social or organization fact, prompt, proactive, or MCP change. Focused backend tests: 9 passed. - Human People R2/R3 gate, stage 2: manual exact-email bootstrap works on real data. After explicit first-party binding, the rooted Person shows the confirmed email as a known contact and route, the Person card/recent-communication count becomes nonzero, the R2 recent-Flow section populates from existing exact-identity attribution, and salience updates from the existing derived inputs. This validates R3 as a recovery/bootstrap affordance, not as the intended primary identity-acquisition workflow. The human explicitly identified the broader product requirement: one Person may have multiple provider identities (email addresses, Telegram, Mattermost, Teams, etc.), and Secretary should derive candidate endpoints from already-ingested Flow rather than require manual entry provider by provider. Current code already extracts provider-neutral source identity evidence from stored email/chat Objects and can expose conservative candidates, but durable attachment remains intentionally gated by exact ownership or explicit confirmation and the enrichment planner is not a general background auto-merge mechanism. Next People direction should therefore focus on a provider-neutral source-derived identity discovery/review flow before durable social/organization relationship ontology. No next task is authorized by this note alone.
- People R4 implemented at `01f978a6b5a7932bbf4551ede5681cd2083c3813`. A rooted Person detail now projects source-derived candidates from the existing `find_identity_candidates(..., include_quarantined_telegram=True)` result. Candidates are no longer mixed into known `identities`. Each candidate keeps provider, identity type, realm, canonical value, display value, confirmable, state `candidate` or `conflicted`, the existing reasons, assessment resolution when present, and at most three source Flow previews. A preview is an active same-user Object already named by `source_object_ids`, with object id, kind, provider, title, and occurred time, and no message body. A missing, hidden, or other-user Object is omitted. The existing candidate cap of 5 and its truncation flag are preserved. Active `user_rejected` evidence that has no attached PersonIdentity row appears as a bounded reversible rejected proposal. Overview and search do not call candidate discovery or source-preview lookup. Reading does not create a PersonIdentity or a Person↔Flow edge and does not call `PersonEnrichmentService.plan()`. Confirm, reject, and retract stay on the existing identity-correction path. Confirm attaches an unowned exact identity and moves it to known contacts. Another Person's ownership stays conflicted and is not reassigned. Reject removes the active candidate. Retract removes only the rejection, after which ordinary discovery may propose it again. Email stays provider-neutral (`identity_type=email`, `provider=email`). The extractor is unchanged: recipient-only email, a Teams non-user sender, and a Mattermost message without a server realm stay outside candidates; a Telegram group sender is still extracted and is proposed only when the existing name match applies. First-party People include a stored private Telegram candidate while the default model-facing candidate read stays gated. The rooted UI shows «Известные контакты», «Возможные контакты» when candidates or truncation exist, and «Отклонённые предложения» when reversible rejections exist. Explanations come from existing reasons: name similarity, exact identifier, identity conflict, or a neutral stored-message fallback. Source rows open the existing object detail. «Подтвердить» is shown only when confirmable. «Это не этот человек» rejects. A conflict is visible and has no confirm action. «Добавить email» remains the manual fallback. Empty candidates add no section. Overview does not show the review controls. - People PP1 implemented at `d9437f6b8ece61e01865ae630dc0e9f196e87cc8`. People overview now derives at most 5 promotion candidates from stored direct contacts inside the existing 90-day / 400-row scan. A candidate requires a normalized exact identity, at least 2 qualifying direct hits, no active Person owner, and no active suppression. Public/group exposure does not qualify. Email, Mattermost DM, Teams one-on-one, and private Telegram use the existing direction and identity normalizers. First-party People reads may include quarantined Telegram; the model-facing gate and `TELEGRAM_MTPROTO_AI_ENABLED` are unchanged. Candidate reads write nothing. `POST /graph/people/promotions` approves, suppresses, or retracts. Approval creates one Person, attaches that exact identity, and records active `user_confirmed` evidence. A repeated approval of the same promotion returns that Person. An identity already owned another way returns conflict and leaves no orphan Person. Suppression is one active row per exact identity in `person_promotion_feedback` and does not suppress a different endpoint with a similar display name. Retract makes a still-qualifying identity visible again. The overview shows «Предлагаемые люди» and «Скрытые предложения» only when those lists exist. Rooted Person detail does not show them. Manual Person creation remains. No Person-to-Flow edge, relationship, Organization, Task actor, provider lookup, or model call was added. - Architect review of People PP1 implementation `d9437f6b8ece61e01865ae630dc0e9f196e87cc8`: NOT YET SOURCE READY. Git refs/HOLD/migration state are correct, but PP1-H1 blocker found in Mattermost direct-contact eligibility. Current PP1 treats any `channel_type=D` Mattermost post as a remote direct contact and extracts `author_user_id`, while Mattermost history stores both remote and current-user-authored DM posts and the normalized Object metadata has no direction field. Because the connected `MattermostAccount` already stores the same-user `remote_user_id`, two outgoing DM posts authored by the user can currently promote the user themself as an unresolved Person candidate. Required correction: for Mattermost promotion, resolve the stored `account_id` to a same-user `MattermostAccount`, require realm/server consistency, and fail closed unless `author_user_id` is proven different from that account's `remote_user_id`. Missing/malformed/cross-user account context must not qualify. No architecture change, no new migration, no production deploy.
- People PP1-H1 implemented at `07bd8bafdb2f53a6a8475fc2d792687fa373a149`. A stored Mattermost DM qualifies for assisted promotion only when `channel_type` is `D`, `account_id` resolves to a `MattermostAccount` owned by the same Secretary user, the message `server_url` matches that account's server, and `author_user_id` differs from that account's `remote_user_id`. Missing, malformed, cross-user, or server-inconsistent account facts contribute no hit. The user's own DM posts do not become a candidate, and approval of that identity creates no Person. Email, Teams one-on-one, private Telegram, quarantine, ranking, the 90-day / 400-row scan, suppression, and approval of a real remote identity are unchanged. No migration was added. Repository Alembic head remains `0051`. Production remains `9c9f0b0e72ffa7cf4ba74fcee60f7f05aeb6dffa`, Alembic `0050`. Client source did not change, so the Linux debug bundle was not rebuilt and remains `/tmp/secretary-pp1-W3J80y/secretary-prerelease/client/build/linux/x64/debug/bundle/personal_secretary`. - Human PP1 gate on exact HG1 client: PARTIAL FUNCTIONAL PASS / UX FAIL. Real production suggestions are present; the user reports that many suggested people are useful and many are correctly dismissible, and has already exercised both Add and Do-not-suggest actions on real data. This proves the assisted-promotion loop is live. However the current People overview review surface is not acceptable: candidates and their source Flow rows visually merge into one continuous text stream, source rows consume excessive vertical space, and the unbounded promotion Column is inserted before the Expanded graph canvas. With several candidates this causes Flutter bottom overflow and can make the People graph effectively inaccessible until proposals are processed. The human requirement is that promotion review remain auxiliary to the People graph: visually distinct person cards, compact clickable source tiles, bounded independently scrollable/collapsible review area, and graph access preserved regardless of current suggestion volume. Because the user has already used Do-not-suggest, production `person_promotion_feedback` may now contain real user feedback; no destructive 0051->0050 rollback may be assumed safe without the existing emptiness proof.
- Human People design direction after PP1 HG1: expand HG2 from an overflow-only patch into the first usable People workspace visual baseline. The People overview should use compact Person-specific summary cards (person icon/avatar placeholder, strong name, provider cues, factual task/communication metrics) while rooted Person focus should present existing canonical task involvement and recent body-free Flow as compact responsive tiles. Promotion review uses the same card/tile language, remains bounded/collapsible, and must never block graph access. Global social/organization roles such as manager/colleague/company remain deliberately absent until separately confirmed Person Knowledge exists; task-specific actor roles may be shown only on their corresponding task tiles. This remains a client-only slice with no backend/schema/production change.
- Future People UX hypothesis to evaluate after PP1-HG2 human review: keep the People graph as the permanent primary surface and reuse a right-side inspector drawer, analogous to the Task graph detail pane. Contextual social/authority knowledge (manager/director/colleague/family/client) remains desirable, but as a late semantic layer rather than the foundation of People layout.
- The later authority/relationship layer is intended to help Secretary prioritization, especially hands-free summaries such as “what is important?”. It must be treated as one strong contextual input, not a deterministic priority rule: a director/manager message may deserve earlier review, but deadlines, explicit waiting state, task dependency, message content, and other relevance evidence remain independent signals.

Verify that the mature Person/Task ontology can be consumed lazily and boundedly without dumping the People graph or large relationship context into every model call. Measure prompt/tool budget and check whether the added ontology improves Secretary decisions rather than increasing noise.

- People PC1 implemented at `47df2709472fc120cb78e61d2fe309a455bdd636`. Two distinct active same-user Person objects can be previewed and then merged only after an explicit confirmation. The preview is read-only and blocks the merge when the duplicate has an active incident edge that is not an incoming Task→Person actor role (`requested_by`, `delegated_to`, `waiting_on`, `involves`), or when identities, evidence, or actor edges exceed the caps 32 / 64 / 32. Apply runs in one transaction: exact identities move through `PersonIdentityService.reassign`, active evidence is copied or reused on the survivor with provenance preserved, and the four Task actor roles are copied only when the survivor does not already have that task/role fact. The survivor title is unchanged. The duplicate is tombstoned only after those transfers, its bookmark is removed, and a bounded `person_consolidation` audit is stored on the duplicate. Undo restores the duplicate, moves the recorded identities back, retracts only merge-created evidence, and removes only merge-created actor edges. If later evidence or actor state cannot be distinguished, undo fails closed and leaves the merge in place. Repeating the same merge is idempotent; merging an already merged Person into a different survivor is rejected. The People inspector exposes «Объединить с…», a Person-only picker that excludes the current Person, a survivor/duplicate preview with swap, and «Объединить людей». A conflicted identity candidate can open the same preview through «Возможно, это один человек → Объединить» and does not merge by itself. After success the duplicate leaves active People reads, the survivor stays selected, and a safe undo row reads `Объединено: <old title> · Отменить`. - Architect source review of PC1 `47df2709472fc120cb78e61d2fe309a455bdd636`: NOT YET SOURCE READY. Identity transfer, evidence copying, tombstone/audit, idempotence, UI confirmation, and guarded undo are directionally correct, but Task actor preservation has a fail-closed defect. `PersonConsolidationService._actor_fact_exists()` treats any non-rejected survivor edge for the same Task/role as reusable, regardless of state/origin/confidence. Thus a confirmed duplicate role can be silently weakened/lost when survivor has only a proposed or otherwise divergent Task/role fact. Separately, preview classifies an edge from a hidden/tombstoned Task as an allowed actor edge, while apply later silently skips that Task and still tombstones the duplicate, allowing a restored Task to lose its consolidated actor fact. PC1 must never silently drop or weaken explicit Task->Person semantics. Required H1: preview/apply must distinguish an exactly reusable survivor actor fact from a divergent same Task/role fact; divergent facts block merge rather than being reconciled implicitly. Every duplicate actor edge admitted by preview must point from an active same-user Task and must be copied exactly or cause merge to fail; no silent `continue`. Add regressions for confirmed-vs-proposed and hidden Task cases. Also make evidence reuse fail closed if the DB-unique evidence signature matches but payload fields that PC1 promises to preserve (weight/provenance kind/source/explanation/details) differ, rather than silently treating non-equivalent evidence as reusable. No migration and no production deploy.
- Add a dedicated final Graph stabilization / UX semantics pass after the planned Person-graph functional sequence reaches logical completion and before the final LLM/Harness budget audit. The purpose is not cosmetic polish. By then the user intends to populate Tasks/People heavily with real data, so both graph workspaces should be made operationally trustworthy: the same action should live in the same place, primary/secondary information hierarchy should be consistent, scrolling/selection/inspector behavior should be predictable, empty/partial/loading states must not masquerade as semantic zero, relation creation/edit/remove/undo paths must be discoverable, and dense real data must remain navigable without layout traps. Treat this as a bug-fix + UI/UX semantics sweep across Task and People graphs, using real-data human testing to reveal remaining logical defects that would otherwise be masked by interface roughness. Do not start this sweep early and do not let it interrupt the active PC1-H1 / subsequent Person functionality; schedule it once People/Task↔Person/People-Landscape functionality is logically complete.

- Human PC1 exact-release gate: NOT ACCEPTED / H2 REQUIRED. The exact-release client proved merge/preview/apply are reachable, but real-data testing exposed two semantic defects and one safety-critical UX gap. First, after a successful merge the survivor can show a false identity conflict because PersonGraphWorkspaceService conflict detection counts active USER_CONFIRMED evidence still retained on the tombstoned duplicate for audit/undo; historical evidence on an inactive absorbed Person must not create an active conflict. Second, rooted identity-candidate discovery currently surfaces any source identity owned by another Person as identity_conflict without first establishing that the identity is actually relevant to the current Person, so unrelated people can receive a misleading “Возможно, это один человек → Объединить” action. A candidate conflict must require independent deterministic grounding, such as exact same-source co-occurrence with an effective identity already owned by the current Person, or an existing candidate match; ownership by another Person alone is not evidence of duplication. The merge picker also uses generic semantic object search even though People workspace already supports deterministic substring search over Person title plus effective identity canonical/display values, making opaque identities unnecessarily hard to find. PC1 remains open; PT1 is not authorized. Production remains `a3d546b069e7e05d68fd42906c662c3ba34caef5`, Alembic `0051`. Executor must not mutate production Person data; the human tester should use PC1 undo to reverse the test merge before H2 validation.
- People PC1-H2 implemented at `e416b115c5c4c6ba5971538d5af9ba2ef067c11b`. Active identity-conflict truth ignores USER_CONFIRMED evidence that remains on a tombstoned duplicate; that audit row is preserved, and undo restores the prior active conflict. A source identity owned by another Person is a merge suggestion only when the current Person already has active evidence for it or the same message also contains an effective identity the current Person owns. Display-name similarity alone still blocks confirmation and does not offer «Возможно, это один человек → Объединить». The merge picker uses People workspace search over title and effective identity canonical/display values, excludes the current Person, and shows the exact conflict cue plus «Остаётся:» / «Исчезает:» and «Поменять, кто останется». No migration and no production deploy. Repository Alembic head remains `0051`. Backend focused suites from `backend/`: consolidation, identity review, and People workspace — 34 passed. Focused Flutter consolidation tests: 6 passed. Flutter analyze of the touched Dart files: 0 errors and 6 pre-existing infos. Ruff passed. `git diff --check` clean. Production and `origin/production` remain `a3d546b069e7e05d68fd42906c662c3ba34caef5`, Alembic `0051`. PT1, People Landscape, Person removal, Secretary Person context, social roles, graph stabilization, MCP, G3B, and S3 were not started.

- Architect review of PC1-H2 `e416b115c5c4c6ba5971538d5af9ba2ef067c11b`: NOT YET SOURCE READY. H2A active-person conflict truth and H2C deterministic People lookup are directionally correct, but H2B still violates the fail-closed duplicate-grounding contract. In `PersonAssistantService._source_candidates()`, when an identity is owned by another Person, `propose_candidates(identity, identity.display_value)` is still consulted and a match to the current Person is accepted as merge grounding. Because exact ownership resolves to the other Person, the current Person can be returned here via display-name `name_similarity`, which H2 explicitly forbids as a basis for consolidation. Separately, `_grounded_duplicate()` treats any active evidence row as sufficient; Person evidence types include weak/inferential signals such as `name_similarity` and `llm_suggestion`, which must not authorize a merge CTA. Required H2.1: remove display-name proposal grounding from the occupied-identity path and allow duplicate CTA only from explicit/high-trust exact-tuple grounding (at minimum active USER_CONFIRMED or USER_ROUTE_CHOICE for the current Person) or the already implemented deterministic same-source co-occurrence with another effective identity owned by the current Person. Weak/inferential evidence (name_similarity, organization_match, graph_context, llm_suggestion) must not suffice on its own. - Human PT1 gate found a directionality UX blocker in the Person-side role picker. On a real Person card, the tester selected `Поручено` intending “this Person assigned the task to me”. The stored canonical relation is actually `delegated_to` (Task -> Person), which means “this Task is delegated/assigned to this Person”. The resulting Person tile showed `Задача · Делегировано`, confirming the canonical fact was the reverse of the tester's intent. Because PT1 is explicitly about trustworthy relation truth, a control that naturally causes a user to write the opposite actor fact is not acceptable. The test relation should be manually removed by the human tester; Executor must not mutate production Person/Task data. PT1 remains NOT HUMAN-ACCEPTED. Required PT1-H2: make Person-side actor-role selection and post-write labeling directionally explicit relative to the current Person, without changing backend semantics. `requested_by` must read as the Person asking/requesting the task; `delegated_to` as the Task being assigned to the Person; `waiting_on` as waiting on the Person; `involves` as the Person participating. The add dialog should show an unambiguous human-readable meaning before the write (prefer explicit person-relative wording and/or a confirmation summary), and the Person involvement tile must use equally unambiguous wording. No migration, no backend ontology change, no deploy, and People Landscape remains unauthorized until this human gate passes.

PT1-H2 remains required before human acceptance so `requested_by` and `delegated_to` cannot be naturally read in the opposite direction.

- PL1 preflight architecture inspection after PT1 completion: current Task geography is client-derived, not stored truth. `GraphWorkspaceController` computes generic positions, Task mode overlays `projectTaskMapHierarchy(...).positions`, and the hybrid Task renderer may further refine display geometry. Current People overview is explicitly independent: `projectPeopleOverview` lays Person nodes out as a ranked grid, and switching controller mode fetches a separate People workspace after clearing positions. People overview does not currently expose the bounded Task-anchor set needed to derive Person placement from Task geography. Therefore PL1 will not be implemented as one broad UI rewrite. First bounded slice PL1-A will establish a pure deterministic spatial projection contract from existing Task coordinates + explicit Task↔Person participation. It must use an unweighted geometric centroid, deduplicate the same Task when a Person has multiple actor roles, perform deterministic local Person de-collision, omit unanchored People rather than invent coordinates, and persist no Person coordinates. UI/API wiring will follow only after this projection primitive is source-reviewed.
- PL1 design clarification: some valid People will intentionally have no Task anchors for long periods (for example family or future collaborators). Absence of a Task anchor is not missing/corrupt data and must not cause a fabricated position inside Task geography. Preserve two spatial states: **anchored Person** = derived from explicit linked Task coordinates; **unanchored Person** = known Person with no usable Task anchor. PL1-A should continue to omit unanchored People from the derived-coordinate helper exactly as scoped. A later People Landscape UI slice should keep unanchored People visible in a clearly non-semantic peripheral area outside the Task-geography bounds (a deterministic shelf/halo/zone), where placement communicates only “not currently anchored to work”, not proximity, relationship, or hierarchy. When the first usable Task relation appears, the Person should leave that peripheral area and derive position from Task geography. Do not persist these fallback coordinates and do not infer family/social/authority relations from unanchored status.
- People PL1-A implemented at `679c8af09073e768c39fd34dc1d0192706be347d`. `projectPeopleFromTasks` derives Person top-lefts from existing Task top-lefts: one Task keeps that coordinate, several Tasks use the unweighted arithmetic centroid, and the same Task id counts once. A Person with no usable Task anchor is omitted. Overlapping People are separated locally in stable Person-id order using the existing graph node size and overlap check. The helper does not persist coordinates, mutate Task positions, or read role semantics. `projectPeopleOverview`, `GraphWorkspaceScreen`, and `GraphWorkspaceController` are unchanged. - PL1 geography clarification after source inspection: `projectTaskMapHierarchy(...).positions` is the correct canonical Task coordinate layer for People Landscape. In the hybrid Task presentation, Task nodes are fixed at those canonical positions; refinement moves presentation satellites/expanded Flow cards, not Task geography. PL1 should therefore reuse `projectTaskMapHierarchy` for any Task context it receives rather than consume hybrid display coordinates or invent another Task layout.
- PL1 anchor-truth rule: only **confirmed** canonical Task actor edges (`requested_by`, `delegated_to`, `waiting_on`, `involves`) may anchor a Person spatially. Proposed actor relations remain visible for review but are not facts and must not move a Person before confirmation. Rejected, terminal/inactive Task relations and generic graph edges are not spatial anchors. Anchor lists must be complete before centroid projection; a bounded/truncated anchor list is a distinct state from a genuinely unanchored Person and must never be used to compute a partial centroid.
- People PL1-B implemented at `1d6ed13501d1e107855cfde1f8de2b47fe33333e`. Every Person in the People workspace read now carries `landscape_task_ids` and `landscape_task_ids_complete`. Proposed, rejected, generic `related_to`, and inactive or terminal Tasks do not anchor. One shared query covers the People in the result. The cap is `PEOPLE_LANDSCAPE_TASK_ANCHOR_CAP = 64`: 64 or fewer ids are complete, more than 64 returns the first 64 in Task-id order with `complete=false`. An empty complete list means the Person is unanchored. `task_involvement` and `open_task_count` are unchanged. The People screen, `projectPeopleOverview`, and `projectPeopleFromTasks` are not wired to this list. No new endpoint, migration, or production deploy. Repository Alembic head remains `0051`. On real production data, the three currently Task-involved People are present but their cards do not correspond to the expected Task geography: the anchored Person cards appear permuted between the left-lower, right/Samsung-school, and upper/main-work regions. - Second human People Landscape gate (PL1-HG2): CORRECTIONS STILL REQUIRED. The PL1-F identity-keying fix is working at the card layer: People that share the same anchor (for example the two People tied to the same Task in the gate data) are locally separated as one group, and unanchored People are now correctly isolated in the right-side strip. The remaining mismatch is one layer below card identity: the Task-derived base geography used by People is not the same semantic-window geography shown by the Task screen. In the gate data, the Task screen shows the relevant anchor Tasks in one semantic window, while the People workspace currently supplies only the union of the anchors' full constellations via `landscape_task_context`. `projectTaskMapHierarchy` is component-packed and therefore not subset-stable: removing unrelated components from the Task semantic window changes the packed absolute positions of the remaining components. As a result, a Person can be correctly attached to the right Task id yet still appear in a different macro-region from that Task on the Task screen. PL1-F tests did not catch this because they compared People against `projectTaskMapHierarchy` over the same reduced landscape context, not against the actual Task semantic-window membership. Next correction must preserve the Task screen's semantic-window input set and project each relevant Task window independently; coordinates remain derived client-side and are not persisted.

- Architect supersession after PL1-HG2: the unimplemented `PL1-G Task-window geography parity` plan is withdrawn before execution. The new canonical geography model is persisted Task world-space rather than re-running Task packing from whichever read subset happens to be loaded. Each Task will have a presentation-only canonical center coordinate in one shared world coordinate system, stored separately from semantic Object metadata. Ordinary reads, title/body/due/status edits, People reads, and mode switches must not repack existing Task coordinates. A new isolated Task may receive a new free coordinate without moving existing Tasks. A Task<->Task topology mutation that changes the visible map relation structure is the event that may invalidate/recompute the affected geography; deletion/terminal visibility should not opportunistically compact unrelated space. People never own persisted coordinates: one Task anchor maps to that Task center, several distinct anchors map to the unweighted centroid, same Task across roles is deduplicated, and local deterministic anti-overlap remains presentation-only. Complete-empty-anchor People remain in the right-side non-semantic strip. Tasks and People must use the same world-space; pan/zoom is a separate shared client viewport transform, not part of semantic geography. Persist centers rather than card top-lefts so card-size changes do not move geography. Do not store these coordinates in `Object.metadata`; use a dedicated layout projection with revision/version metadata so stale snapshots fail closed and future layout-algorithm changes can invalidate safely. Planned bounded sequence: PL1-G1 persistence/state foundation; PL1-G2 topology invalidation + additive read/write contract; PL1-G3 Task renderer consumes/publishes canonical coordinates; PL1-G4 People consumes the same coordinates and preserves shared viewport; then exact-release human gate. Production remains `666683134797948871266e84fd105f0ca0c43476`, Alembic `0051 / 0051` until an explicitly authorized migration rollout.

A user has one layout state with monotonic `topology_revision`, nullable `snapshot_revision`, and nullable `algorithm_version`. Positions store finite Task center coordinates `world_x` / `world_y` for a snapshot revision. Coordinates are not written to `Object.metadata`. Deleting the user or the Task cascades the layout rows. `TaskLayoutService` reads that state, replaces a complete snapshot only for the expected topology revision, and invalidates topology by incrementing the revision without deleting stored centers. A snapshot is usable only when `snapshot_revision == topology_revision`. Stale revisions, duplicate Task ids, missing ids, foreign-user ids, non-Task ids, and non-finite coordinates fail before any snapshot write. Users cannot read or replace each other's layout. Relation mutations, public API, `GraphWorkspaceOut`, Flutter, `projectTaskMapHierarchy`, and production data are unchanged. Terminal status alone does not remove a Task. `read().usable` is true only when `snapshot_revision == topology_revision` and every current eligible Task has a center in that snapshot. Extra historical centers do not by themselves make the remaining set unusable. `GET /graph/task-layout` and `PUT /graph/task-layout` expose that view; stale revisions are HTTP 409 and validation failures are HTTP 422. `GET /graph/task-layout/topology` returns the current revision, the complete eligible Task objects, and only Task↔Task edges that affect the Tasks map. The caps are 500 Tasks and 2000 edges; overflow is HTTP 422 with no partial payload. Creating, deleting, or changing map participation of an affecting Task↔Task relation increments an existing topology revision once in the same transaction. Proposed `part_of` does not count until confirmed; a visible proposed non-`part_of` edge does not increment again when confirmed; rejecting it does. Actor edges, Task→non-Task evidence, and ordinary Task title/status edits do not increment. Creating a layout-eligible Task increments an existing layout revision and does not create one when no layout state exists yet. Hiding or rejecting a Task does not move the remaining centers. No new migration, Flutter change, coordinate calculation, or production deploy. - Single-world client direction refined after PL1-G2: the product has one spatial graph/world whose canonical anchors are Task centers. Tasks and People are presentation layers over that same world, not separate maps. The current Tasks/People switch should eventually only change which objects are visible while retaining one camera transform (pan/zoom/translation). - PL1-G4 design decision: move unrooted People onto the same canonical Task-center world and preserve one world camera across unrooted Tasks<->People switches. A Person with one confirmed complete Task anchor is centered on that Task center; multiple distinct anchors use the unweighted centroid of Task centers; same Task across roles is deduplicated; deterministic local spread remains presentation-only. Missing/incomplete anchor truth or missing canonical Task center fails the whole People overview closed to the existing People grid. Complete-empty-anchor People remain in a deterministic right-side strip outside the full canonical Task world. Person overview markers should shrink further from the temporary 156x56 cards to a compact glyph+text marker, with display name and at most one explicit short identity cue; rooted Person/inspector remain unchanged. Shared camera means a world point must stay at the same viewport pixel across a healthy unrooted Tasks<->People layer switch; preserving the same raw Matrix4 is insufficient if canvas normalization changes, so the renderer may refactor/compensate its world-to-canvas frame. Mode switch itself must not auto-fit. The optional future combined Tasks+People layer remains deferred.

A Person with one complete anchor is centered on that Task center before local spread; several distinct anchors use their unweighted centroid; a repeated Task id counts once. When the canvas origin changes, the camera translation is compensated by `scale * origin delta`, so a canonical world point stays on the same viewport pixel and the scale is unchanged. - PL1-HG3 human gate: FAILED on real production data. The exact-source bundle shows a large layer-parity offset: after viewing the Task world and switching to People without intentionally changing the camera, single-anchor People do not occupy the corresponding Task screen positions; the People layer is displaced substantially (visually about two Task columns to the right, with additional vertical drift). This disproves the current camera/frame compensation despite the synthetic one-Task camera test passing. Code review explains the blind spot: unrooted Tasks and People still normalize their own scene bounds independently (`hybridPresentationBounds` / `GraphLayout.computeBounds`) and then attempt to compensate with `_holdWorldPoint`; the existing parity test covers only one Task/one Person and no real semantic-window/truncation-frame change. The correction should make the unrooted canonical world frame itself shared/stable, with layer bounds affecting extents/Fit but not world origin, and it must account for any viewport-global offset change so the same canonical world point remains on the same screen pixel.

- The same human gate also rejects the current same-anchor Person anti-overlap semantics. Maksim Yatsenko and netcup Team share the NutrTrack Task anchor but are rendered as if they belonged to substantially different regions. Current `people_landscape.dart` uses a radial placement step based on the marker diagonal (`_minCenterSeparation`), which creates excessive semantic distance even for identical anchors. Same-anchor People should instead form one compact deterministic cluster around their shared anchor: no overlap, only a small visual gap, and the cluster itself remains centered on the shared Task point. Larger displacement is reserved for collisions between genuinely different anchor groups, not for members of the same anchor group.

Healthy unrooted Tasks and People now share one world origin: the top-left of the full canonical Task-card world. A layer's own bounds can grow the canvas to the right and bottom and can change manual Fit, but they do not redefine that origin. `_holdWorldPoint` is gone. - Roadmap reprioritization after PL1:
  1. Immediate: a few bounded Graph UI/UX polish rounds because Graph is now the main working surface for creating/maintaining many Tasks and People. Do not reopen accepted geography/ontology unless a regression is found.
  2. Next major program: Secretary agent/harness/tooling audit and partial refactor. The repo now has substantial independent surfaces under `backend/app/assistant`, `backend/app/llm`, `backend/app/tools`, `backend/app/mcp`, `backend/app/ai_audit`, and `backend/app/proactive`; these predate parts of the current ontology and must be checked against the final Person/Task/Flow/Time model rather than assumed current.
     - Capability audit: can the model discover and correctly use current Task actor roles, Task relations, Person identities, Flow provenance, temporal facts, and explicit unknowns through the actual prompt/context/tool/MCP pipeline?
     - Tool coverage audit: identify ontology facts/actions the model cannot read or mutate cleanly, duplicate/legacy tool paths, stale schemas, and places where provider vocabulary leaks into canonical semantics.
     - Context/token audit: instrument representative secretary scenarios and measure system/prompt payload, tool-schema payload, retrieved context, tool calls, round trips, and model usage. Replace broad preloading with bounded, need-driven retrieval/tool exposure. First measure current baseline, then set explicit quality + token budgets; aim for large reductions where the current pipeline overfeeds context, without sacrificing task success.
     - Benchmark gate: keep a small scenario suite (e.g. “what am I waiting on from X?”, “what should I do today?”, “prepare/follow up a commitment”, “route a message to the right Person/Task”, “explain why something is urgent”) and compare correctness, tool calls, latency, and token cost before/after refactors.
  3. Hands-free/proactive audit and fixes after the Secretary audit, because it depends on the same semantics, tools, confirmations, and context policy. Treat hands-free as a bounded interaction/policy problem, not a separate ontology.
  4. Provider-neutral voice/media Flow ingestion + transcription. Preserve provenance/privacy gates; transcript is evidence/representation, not an authoritative Task by default.
  5. Explicit contextual relationship/authority facts (manager/director/colleague/friend/client/etc.) later. No inference from message count, assignment frequency, titles, or graph position. Optional hierarchical People view may come only after the semantic layer exists.
  6. Further Task Graph refinements remain deferred unless real usage exposes a concrete need.
  7. General visual/design polish should happen in repeated bounded waves across the product rather than one risky rewrite.
  8. Near the end, run two separate broad audits:
     - code/architecture audit: dead and duplicate paths, stale endpoints/contracts, unreachable legacy behavior, oversized/entangled modules, feature flags, migrations, client/backend drift, weak tests, observability gaps, and cleanup candidates;
     - security audit: authn/authz and cross-user isolation, endpoint exposure, secrets/config, logs/PII, connector/MCP/tool boundaries, prompt/tool injection surfaces, upload/media handling, dependency/container/network posture, production ingress/TLS/reverse proxy, backup/restore, data retention, and audit trails.
- Deferred product detail preserved: individual-vs-organization/team glyphs require an explicit grounded entity/display type; never infer this from names such as “Team”, provider, or email domain.

Changed files: `client/lib/graph/relation_target_label.dart`, `client/lib/graph/graph_workspace_screen.dart`, `client/lib/graph/graph_workspace_controller.dart`, `client/test/graph/relation_target_label_test.dart`, `client/test/graph/graph_relation_target_disambiguation_test.dart`, `client/test/graph/graph_task_rename_test.dart`, and `client/test/graph/graph_workspace_screen_test.dart`. In the Add-relation target search, a Task title that is duplicated in the current result set is shown with its confirmed immediate `part_of` parent, for example `Обучение (Основная работа)` and `Обучение (Академическая деятельность)`. The suffix is presentation only. A unique title is unchanged. A duplicate with no confirmed parent shows `(без родителя)`. Proposed and rejected `part_of` are not shown as an established parent. The selected relation still uses the exact Task id. Parent context comes from the existing Task profile read, and only for the duplicated Task ids. A successful title edit from the Graph management panel or from «Подробнее» replaces the current node immediately in both the unrooted Tasks overview and the rooted Task view. The old title disappears. Mode, root, selection, semantic-window index, and the canonical Task center stay. The in-place edit does not fetch the workspace again and does not recompute or replace Task layout. An in-place Task update made inside «Подробнее» also skips the previous return-time workspace reload, so a stale overview payload cannot put the old title back. Delete and other non-preserving detail exits still refresh. Remaining limitation: two duplicates that share one confirmed parent, or that both lack one, still share the same visible suffix; returning from the full-screen details route can shift camera translation through the existing shared-world viewport compensation while scale stays. The in-panel rename does not move the camera matrix. - GFX-B implemented at `cf812130c59c45eee25a3a96720c0f0fe99b3c29`. Changed files: `client/lib/graph/task_part_of_connect.dart`, `client/lib/graph/graph_workspace_screen.dart`, and `client/test/graph/graph_part_of_drag_test.dart`. Tasks mode shows four mid-edge handles on a Task card when that card is hovered or selected, and on visible Task cards while a connection drag is active. People mode, Person nodes, and non-Task nodes do not show them. The card keeps its canonical size and canvas origin; the handle frame is padded by the 7px radius and the positioned origin is shifted back by the same amount. Dragging a handle draws one temporary solid directed line from the handle toward the pointer. The preview is presentation only. Dropping on another visible Task card, including its handle, sends exactly one existing relation request with the dragged Task as `source_id`, the drop Task as `target_id`, and `type` `part_of`. Success goes through `applyCreatedRelation` and the existing topology refresh, so a successful `part_of` may refit the camera. - AH2-T implemented at `aa3f475a3a0ee49b938364e6d53f3657711b1a9b`. `create_task` and `update_task` write `planned_start_at` and `planned_end_at` as one planned execution interval. Both boundaries must be present together. Create with both omitted, or both null, stores no interval. Update with both omitted leaves the interval unchanged. A non-null pair replaces it. Both null clears it. End must be after start. The same pair and a clear of an already empty interval are `changed=false`. The pair is normalized with `normalize_tool_datetime` and validated by `validate_planned_execution_interval`. `TaskMutationService.patch_task_fields` passes the whole pair to `ObjectUpdate`. `PATCH /tasks/{id}` uses the same presence rules. Assistant and MCP expose the same field names and no aliases. MCP writes still require approval. A malformed pair fails validation before approval and before any Task or edge is created. The interval is not `due_at`, a reminder, or calendar busy time, and it does not change lifecycle or layout. The static AH1 `MODEL_GAP` is closed in `docs/ontology_harness_parity_audit.md` and `docs/SECRETARY_TOOLSET_MATRIX.md`. Eval scenario M2 was added. Model behavior stays `BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED` until AH2-M. No Alembic migration. Pytest: 45 passed (`test_ah2t_planned_interval.py`, `test_ah2d_task_tool_descriptions.py`, `test_assistant_ontology_kernel.py`, `test_domain_tools.py`, `test_ah1_doc_registry_drift.py`, `test_task_relations.py`, and `test_task_completion_mode.py` except the unrelated `0050` migration rewrite). Production was not deployed and remains `0719e9bf5af75a8065a9916d8e27c0247a3921ec`, Alembic `0052 / 0052`. Rollout and model verification are pending. AH2-E, AH2-M, and AH2-C were not started.
- AH2-T SOURCE-ACCEPTED on 2026-10-01. Implementation `aa3f475a3a0ee49b938364e6d53f3657711b1a9b`, Executor HOLD `863ff150d1006f32105a344c37e6806bc4e7c34e`. Source review confirmed one-pair planned interval semantics across `CreateTaskInput`, `UpdateTaskInput`, `TaskMutationService.patch_task_fields`, `PATCH /tasks/{id}`, Assistant contracts, and MCP. Omission leaves an existing interval unchanged; update with both null clears; a non-null pair requires end > start; setting the same pair or clearing an already-empty interval is a truthful `changed=false` no-op. Datetimes pass through the existing normalization path. Malformed pairs fail before approval/mutation. The interval remains distinct from `due_at`, reminders, and calendar busy time. 45 focused tests passed, including input validation, create persistence, canonical set/replace/clear/no-op, pure tool update, REST parity, Assistant/MCP parity, fail-before-approval, and MCP forwarding. The AH1 planned-interval `MODEL_GAP` is closed at static contract/domain level; behavior remains `BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED`. No migration was added. Production remains `0719e9bf5af75a8065a9916d8e27c0247a3921ec`, Alembic `0052 / 0052`; AH2-P/D/T are not deployed yet. Next: AH2-E executable deterministic eval harness, then a reviewed schema-neutral rollout before AH2-M real-model evaluation.
- AH2-E implemented at `b30f739ee5c8e6bf8cf264f63c9f4e5e73b15f06`. The harness lives in `backend/evals/secretary_agent` and does not change `backend/app`. `RecordingToolRunner` wraps the same `__call__(tool_name, arguments)` contract as the Assistant loop, records the call before the result, keeps a copy of the original arguments, forwards `commit_model_visible_outputs()` when the delegate has it, and re-raises exceptions after recording them. The scorer checks tool order, one-of alternatives, exact relation type and direction through symbolic ids, actor fields, forbidden tools, mutation bounds, approval before execution, no execution while pending, exact edge id after `list_neighbors`, no-op versus changed, and scenario final facts. Statuses are `PASS`, `FAIL`, `NOT_APPLICABLE`, and `MANUAL_REVIEW`. A `FAIL` fails the scenario. A `MANUAL_REVIEW` makes it `INCOMPLETE` and is not a pass. User-visible answer text is always `MANUAL_REVIEW`. No LLM-as-judge and no product-database persistence. The catalogue has the fifteen documented ids. A done existing Task may still `create_task` under T2, and a chat F2 uses `send_message`; those are extra scorers, not extra headings. CLI: `python -m evals.secretary_agent.cli score <run.json>` and `validate-catalog`. Positive evidence: one golden per id passes the automatable dimensions and stays `INCOMPLETE`. Negative evidence covers finite T1, duplicate open T2, F1 create or `related_to`, M1 task instead of reminder, M2 due-only or one planned boundary, reversed or `depends_on` R1, R2 `related_to`, R3 invented edge or remove without neighbors, A1 `changed=true`, A2 pending treated as executed, S1 mutation, P1 send before ambiguity is resolved, and N1 mutation on a bare name. Pytest: 56 passed (`test_ah2e_eval_harness.py` 38, plus `test_ah1_doc_registry_drift.py`, `test_assistant_ontology_kernel.py`, `test_ah2d_task_tool_descriptions.py`, and `test_ah2t_planned_interval.py`). Limitation: a harness pass is not evidence a model chooses the right trace, and AH1 `BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED` stays unverified until AH2-M. No model was called. Production was not deployed and remains `0719e9bf5af75a8065a9916d8e27c0247a3921ec`, Alembic `0052 / 0052`. AH2-M was not started.
- AH2-E SOURCE-ACCEPTED on 2026-10-01. Implementation `b30f739ee5c8e6bf8cf264f63c9f4e5e73b15f06`, Executor HOLD `ea2c2a90202aa1357b5b19e283a4fe932c2e7aef`. Source review confirmed the harness is isolated under `backend/evals/secretary_agent` and does not modify `backend/app` runtime. The serializable run format records scenario id, ordered tool calls, arguments, status/approval/execution/effect facts, symbolic-id map, final semantic facts, and user-visible answer without chain-of-thought. `RecordingToolRunner` forwards calls and `commit_model_visible_outputs()` unchanged while recording a deep copy of model arguments and tool outcomes. The deterministic scorer covers required/forbidden tools, ordering, alternatives, relation direction/type, actor fields, planned interval, mutation bounds, approval stage vs execution, exact edge identity, no-op effects, symbolic provenance, and final-state facts. Free-form answer truthfulness is always `MANUAL_REVIEW`, so a structurally correct golden is `INCOMPLETE`, not falsely passed. Catalogue drift checks bind 15 documented ids to registry tools, canonical generic relations, and Task actor roles. 56 tests passed, including 38 harness tests plus preserved AH1/AH2-P/D/T contract tests. No model was called and AH1 behavior verdicts remain `BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED`. Production remains `0719e9bf5af75a8065a9916d8e27c0247a3921ec`, Alembic `0052 / 0052`. Next gate: explicit human authorization for one schema-neutral production rollout containing AH2-P + AH2-D + AH2-T before AH2-M real-model evaluation; AH2-E itself is eval-only and need not be deployed for product behavior.

- REL1A implemented at `e7f1e79dd83e3007bc97a6dff7aa1bd083128d93`. Person roles are an emergent per-user vocabulary, not a closed dictionary, Organization, Person-to-Person edge, Task actor role, or importance score. Alembic `0053` adds `person_role_terms` (`user_id`, `display_text`, `normalized_key`, unique per user) and `person_role_assignments` (person, term, optional context, `origin`, `state=active|retracted`, provenance, nullable `source_object_id`). Normalization trims and collapses whitespace and case-folds only the key; the first accepted display text is kept. `Директор` / ` директор ` / `ДИРЕКТОР` reuse one term. `директор` and `генеральный директор` stay distinct. No stemming, translation, edit distance, embeddings, or synonym merge. The same role with no context and with `Arenadata` are different assignments; context case and whitespace collapse only trivial duplicates. A repeated active assignment is idempotent. Retract keeps the row and the term. At most 16 active assignments per Person; retract frees a slot. Blank or oversize text is rejected. Cross-user, non-Person, rejected, and deleted targets fail closed. Search is `GET /graph/person-role-terms`. Assign is `POST /graph/people/{person_id}/roles`. Retract is `DELETE /graph/people/{person_id}/roles/{assignment_id}`. People workspace projects active assignments in one batched query. The People detail section `Роли` suggests existing terms, offers `Создать роль «…»` only when the typed text is not an exact lexical match, and takes an optional context. Person cards show the first two distinct role titles and an overflow count, without raw ids. Personal relevance, proactive ranking, Assistant tools, and MCP were not changed. - REL1C-B implemented at `22a4ac91e52bc3097e8945dc3223fdc0adaa7ba9`. Assistant Person-role writes are exactly `assign_person_role` (`INTERNAL_WRITE`) and `retract_person_role` (`DESTRUCTIVE_INTERNAL_WRITE`). Both are Assistant-exposed, not MCP-exposed, have a prepare method and frozen execution input, and return `approval_required` without writing. The same role plus the same context is a no-op and does not rewrite manual provenance. A different context is a separate assignment. The active cap stays 16. Retract requires the exact `(person_id, assignment_id)` pair exposed this turn. It keeps the RoleTerm and the assignment row, and a later execution of an already retracted assignment is `changed=false`. A hidden Person before approval fails closed. New Assistant assignments use `origin=agent`, `provenance_kind=assistant_action_plan`, and a bounded `aap:<operation_id>` key. Manual assignments stay `origin=user` and `user_manual`. Approval cards show the Person title, role, optional context, and `reuse_existing` or `create_if_missing`. Finalization treats `changed=false` as no add or removal, and role text plus context stay untrusted data. Role writes still take `lock_user_serialization_row`. They do not route through labels, Task actor fields, generic relations, or Person identity.
